### document-05

**Undo/Redo during a transient (IME preview) revision records the uncommitted preview text as a redo/undo entry**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichDocument.cpp:743` · bug · minor · confirmed (: confirmed; : confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: ReplaceTransient (608-611) changes `source_` without touching undo\_/redo\_ and the Document keeps no 'transient in progress' state. Document::Undo (743-756) unconditionally pushes `{source_, selection_}` to redo\_ and restores undo\_.back(); Document::Redo mirrors it. If Undo runs while the current revision is a transient preview, the preview text becomes a redo entry and the restored state skips the entry text the header asks callers to restore first (RichDocument.h:117-120). RichTextEditor::Undo/Redo (RichTextEditor.cpp:693-706) forward straight to the document with only an Editable() check, so the only protection is TSF intercepting Ctrl+Z during composition, which is not a library guarantee.
- **Failure**: Document 'base' (one recorded edit from ''), ReplaceTransient(L"base仮") for an active composition, then Undo(): undo\_ restores '' and redo\_ now holds 'base仮'. Redo() republishes the never-committed composition string 'base仮' as document text while the IME/TSF state thinks the composition was cancelled; the entry text 'base' is no longer reachable through history.
- **Fix**: Track the last recorded snapshot in Document (e.g., `SourceSnapshot committed_`) and have Undo/Redo either refuse (return false) while `!source_.IsSameRevision(committed_)` or first restore `committed_` transiently; document the rule in RichDocument.h and add a test to Scope.Tests.RichTextInput.

### document-12

**ChangedRangeFrom's common-ancestor search is O(128^2) over two zero-initialised 5 KiB stack arrays although identities are monotone along a lineage**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichDocument.cpp:497` · simplification · minor · confirmed (: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: ChangedRangeFrom copies up to 128 edits for each snapshot into `std::array<SnapshotEdit, 128> currentEdits{}, previousEdits{}` (line 490, value-initialised = 10 KiB zeroed per call) and then runs a nested loop (497-511) comparing every previous-chain identity with every current-chain identity: up to 129 x 129 = 16,641 comparisons. Because SnapshotLineage::AllocateIdentity is strictly increasing and a parent is always allocated before its child, both chains are strictly decreasing newest-to-oldest, so a merge walk (advance the chain whose head is larger until heads are equal or a chain ends) finds the same lowest common ancestor in O(128). [...]
- **Failure**: Before: after 100 edits on a branch and 100 on the layout's retained snapshot, each of the ~5 calls per keystroke does ~10,000 comparisons plus zeroing 10 KiB; after: ~200 comparisons, no zeroing needed since only `used` entries are read.
- **Fix**: Replace the nested loop with a two-pointer merge over the newest-first arrays using identity ordering; drop the `{}` value-initialisation of the arrays (only entries \< count are read).
- **Verifier (, confirmed)**: Replace the copy-then-nested-scan in ChangedRangeFrom (DxUi.RichDocument.cpp:489-511) with an in-place cursor over each immutable Revision's pages (active entries newest-first, then sealed[2], sealed[1], sealed[0]) that yields identities newest-first, and merge-walk the two cursors by identity (advance the one whose head is larger) until they meet or either ends; then re-walk the found prefix of each cursor for the existing accumulate loops (539-546). ### document-14

**Sizing calls to GetLineMetrics and HitTestTextRange ignore their HRESULT, silently producing zero lines / empty selection on unexpected failure**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichTextLayout.cpp:304` · bug · minor · refuted (: refuted; : refuted) · plan: not scheduled (refuted)

- **What**: `layout->GetLineMetrics(nullptr, 0, &count);` (line 304) and `state_->layout->HitTestTextRange(..., nullptr, 0, &count);` (line 412) discard the HRESULT. The expected result is E\_NOT\_SUFFICIENT\_BUFFER, but any other failure (E\_INVALIDARG from a bad range, device/font loading failure) leaves count == 0: PrepareOwned then publishes `lines == {0}`, `positionedExactly == false` (310) and a layout whose LineBoundary/VisibleRange are wrong, while Range returns no rectangles. Every other DirectWrite call in the file goes through Check() and WIL, which the contract requires for failure visibility (docs/rich-text.md:403-405).
- **Failure**: A font-fallback failure that makes GetLineMetrics fail outright on one chunk: the chunk is published with one phantom line, the Placement phase marks the document inexact and silently switches to whole geometry, hiding the underlying DirectWrite error from the host's diagnostics.
- **Fix**: Capture the HRESULT and `Check` it unless it equals E\_NOT\_SUFFICIENT\_BUFFER (or HRESULT\_FROM\_WIN32(ERROR\_INSUFFICIENT\_BUFFER)); treat count == 0 after S\_OK as a hard error for GetLineMetrics.
- **Verifier (, refuted)**: The failure scenario is unreachable because of ordering guards the reviewer missed. GetMetrics is what forces DirectWrite's full analysis (shaping, font fallback, line breaking); [...]

### document-15

**Each insertion allocates its own immutable buffer, so sequential typing never coalesces and one piece plus ~1 KiB leaf node is retained per keystroke**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichDocument.cpp:713` · performance · minor · confirmed (: confirmed; : confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: ReplaceImpl creates `insertion.buffer = std::make_shared<const std::wstring>(text)` for every changed edit (713), and AppendCoalesced (158-166) only merges fragments when `fragments[used-1].buffer == fragment.buffer && bufferStart + length == fragment.bufferStart`, i.e. same buffer object. Consecutive typed characters therefore always produce new pieces: N keystrokes yield N pieces (bounded only by the 64 Ki cap), N buffer allocations, N new leaf nodes of `std::variant<std::array<Piece,32>, std::array<Child,32>>` (~1 KiB each, copied on every boundary-path rewrite) and N Revision objects (~1.4 KiB with the inline 32-edit page). All of these are retained through the 128-entry undo/redo histories and any held snapshots. [...]
- **Failure**: Typing a 20,000-character note: ~20,000 pieces (625 leaves, height 3), roughly 20,000 x (buffer + leaf + Revision) ~ 50 MB of allocations over the session with ~128 x ~3 KiB retained, and every Text()/Slice over the whole document appends 20,000 one-character views.
- **Fix**: Give Document a bounded, append-only 'add buffer' (e.g. chunks of 4 KiB shared\_ptr\<const std::wstring> that are never mutated once a snapshot references a prefix, or a std::deque-like immutable chunk list) so consecutive inserts at the previous insertion end land in the same buffer and coalesce; keep the current path for non-sequential edits. Measure piece counts in the storage resource test.
- **Verifier (, confirmed)**: A user types a 20,000-character note character by character in RichTextEditor (OnChar -> ReplaceSelection per key, or TSF commits narrowed to one unit). Each keystroke runs Document::ReplaceImpl, which allocates a new one-character shared buffer (:713), rebuilds the right-boundary path of the piece tree (one new ~1 KiB leaf plus one ~1 KiB node per level, height ~3 at this size), allocates a ~1.4 KiB Revision and copies the undo vector. ### document-17

**AllocateIdentity uses a CAS loop with an unreachable 2^64 exhaustion exception**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichDocument.cpp:368` · simplification · minor · confirmed (: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: SnapshotLineage::AllocateIdentity (368-377) loads, checks for `std::numeric_limits<std::uint64_t>::max()` and throws `std::length_error`, then compare\_exchange\_weak-loops. A 64-bit counter cannot be exhausted in any realistic process lifetime, the atomic is needed only because copied Documents share a lineage, and a plain `fetch_add(1, relaxed)` returns the same unique value with less code and no throwing path to reason about inside the Revision constructor's staging contract.

- **Fix**: Replace with fetch\_add; keep `std::atomic` only if copied Documents on different threads remain supported, and say so in a comment.

### document-18

**Builder::Inline searches for the closing marker past the end of the current line**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichMarkdownProjection.cpp:90` · simplification · minor · confirmed (: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: `const auto close = result.source.find(marker, cursor + marker.size());` (line 90) scans the whole remaining source and only afterwards rejects matches with `close < last` (91). In whole-document Project() an unmatched marker on a line therefore scans to the next occurrence of that marker anywhere in the (up to 64 Ki) document instead of stopping at the line end; total work is amortised linear per marker kind but the scan is pointless and the code reads as if matches could span lines, which the v0 grammar forbids (lines are split at 124-126).
- **Failure**: Source: one line starting with a lone backtick followed by 60,000 units of backtick-free text: Project() scans ~60,000 units to conclude the backtick is literal, where a line-bounded search stops at the first EOL.
- **Fix**: Search within `std::wstring_view(result.source).substr(0, last)` (i.e. `find(marker, cursor + size)` on the bounded view) and drop the `close < last` test.
- **Verifier (, confirmed)**: The reviewer's scenario is imprecise: a single 60,000-unit line starting with a lone backtick would be scanned to its end by a line-bounded search too. ### editor-01

`src/Controls/DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp:1557` · bug · major · confirmed (: confirmed; : confirmed) · plan: W1.4

Both in-layout branches of MoveCaret write `state.document.SetSelection(...)` directly (line 1557 semantic branch, line 1595 v0 branch) and then only RequestInvalidate(); they never run that check. Only the no-layout fallback (line 1510) goes through the public SetSelection. So Up/Down/Left/Right/Home/End/PgUp/PgDn can move the caret into another paragraph while `_state->sourceEditing` stays set. [...]
- **Failure**: RichMarkdown document: a bullet list item followed by a fenced code block. Press F2 inside the fence (literal presentation begins), then press Up until the caret is in the list item, then press Enter: OnChar inserts a raw "\\r\\n" instead of continuing the list marker, Ctrl+B does nothing, Backspace at the item boundary deletes a raw grapheme instead of joining. The envelope is only dropped by the first edit (UpdateSemanticSourceRegion sees a change outside the range), so exactly that first keystroke misbehaves. [...]
- **Fix**: Extract the envelope check into a private `void LeaveSourceRegionIfOutside() noexcept` (or a `CommitSelection(Selection, site)` helper) and call it from both MoveCaret branches after SetSelection (before RequestInvalidate), or route MoveCaret through SelectionChanged(false) and re-apply preferredCaretX afterwards. ### editor-03

**IME preview imports discard the whole retained layout graph instead of invalidating it**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp:1367` · performance · major · confirmed (: confirmed; : confirmed) · plan: W1.4

- **What**: ImportTextInputState's non-notifying path (composition previews, cancellation restores) calls RetireLayout() when the text changed. RetireLayout performs `_state->layout.Clear()` (line 810), which for the v0 engine resets `state_` (DxUi.RichDocumentLayout.cpp:197-203). PreparedDocumentLayout::BeginPreparation can only reuse unchanged chunks when `state_` still exists (`compatible = state_ && ...`, `if (compatible && change.kind != FullReplacement && ! state_->chunks.empty()) phase = Reuse`, RichDocumentLayout.cpp:235-263). The committed-edit path Changed() deliberately uses `_state->layout.Invalidate()` with the comment 'retain bounded immutable chunks for the next owner-thread preparation' (lines 728-733). [...]
- **Failure**: Source/v0 view of a 60 Ki-unit document, Japanese IME composition: each preview keystroke imports via ImportTextInputState(host, state, false) -> RetireLayout -> Clear. The next preparation rebuilds all ~60 chunks (one DirectWrite layout each) at the default budget of 4 layouts per turn, i.e. ~15 host turns per keystroke, versus 1-2 chunk rebuilds with Invalidate. Visible composition lag on large documents; the test 'undo reuses matching native geometry' (Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichTextEditor.cpp:433) shows reuse is the intended behaviour for tracked edits.
- **Fix**: Make the preview path mirror Changed()'s invalidation: Invalidate the layout, cancel markdown/semantic staging, reset the rejection records and request preparation, without Clear(). ### editor-04

**Each image completion retires and fully rebuilds the semantic geometry**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp:174` · performance · minor · confirmed (: confirmed; : confirmed) · plan: W3.4

- **What**: CompleteImageRequest calls RetireLayout() (Clear) whenever the resource snapshot identity changes, which is every accepted completion. PreparedSemanticGeometry::Begin's `same()` predicate compares `state.imageResources == images` by shared\_ptr identity (DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:935-951), so a new snapshot always creates a brand-new Preparation that re-shapes every paragraph, marker and table. The sample loader marshals completions one owner message at a time, so a document with N image sources is laid out N+1 times, each pass costing the full bounded budget of turns (and a frame without paint per turn on the window host).
- **Failure**: A document with 32 distinct local images (the maximum) and 4,000 paragraphs: 32 CompleteImageRequest calls arrive one per owner message; each one calls RetireLayout, PrepareContent re-runs the whole IndexElements/Layouts pipeline (4 layouts per turn), i.e. roughly 32 x (paragraphs / 4) host turns before the final frame, during which the window host presents nothing (PreparePendingPageLayout returns false).
- **Fix**: Coalesce completions: have CompleteImageRequest only update the snapshot and RequestContentPreparation(); let PrepareContent compare `state.images.Snapshot()` with the snapshot the current layout was prepared from and rebuild once per owner turn, so completions that land in one message batch share one rebuild. Longer term, let PreparedSemanticGeometry::Begin reuse paragraph boxes whose image set is unchanged (only image elements depend on the snapshot).
- **Verifier (, confirmed)**: Rich mode, 32 distinct local image sources (MaximumSources), up to 4,096 admitted paragraphs (RichSemanticDocument.h:98) within the source cap. ### editor-06

**Paint computes the full selection geometry and re-walks all elements per visible selection line**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp:1412` · performance · minor · confirmed (: confirmed; : confirmed) · plan: W2.7

- **What**: Paint asks `projection.SourceRange(selection.Start(), selection.End())` for the entire selection (not clipped to the viewport) and then, for every rectangle that intersects the viewport, calls `state.layout.Draw(..., rectangle.top, rectangle.bottom, ...)`. PreparedSemanticGeometry::Draw iterates every element and every box each call (DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:1675-1692) and re-issues DrawTextLayout for each intersecting paragraph clipped to that one line. Cost per paint is O(selectionLines) for the range query plus O(visibleSelectionLines x (elements + boxes)) for the walk, repeated on every caret blink.
- **Failure**: Select All on a 64 Ki document with ~2,000 visual lines and ~30 K semantic elements: every repaint (twice per second while focused) allocates ~2,000 selection rectangles, then for the ~50 visible lines walks ~34 K element/box entries each (~1.7 M checks) and draws the intersecting paragraph layout ~50 times.
- **Fix**: Clamp the range query to the visible source range (VisibleSourceRange of the scrolled viewport) before computing rectangles, then draw the selected pass once per box: push one clip geometry (union of that box's selection rectangles, or a PushLayer with a geometry mask) and call Draw once for the viewport. Keep the per-rectangle fill as is.
- **Verifier (, confirmed)**: Semantic (Rich) view, focused editable editor, Ctrl+A on a document near the caps (64Ki units, ~4,000 paragraph boxes, several thousand inline Link/Math elements, ~2,000 visual lines), viewport showing ~50 lines. ### editor-18

**OnMouseDown takes focus before it knows the press can be honoured**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp:2002` · bug · minor · confirmed (: confirmed; : confirmed) · plan: W1.4

- **What**: OnMouseDown calls host.SetFocusControl(this) first, then HitSource(host, point), which throws std::logic\_error whenever the layout is not current or any host content preparation is pending (RequireCurrentLayout, 1092). The catch returns false, so the press is reported unhandled after focus has already moved; the embedded host then neither captures nor remembers the press (src/Rendering/Embedded.cpp:659-676) while the editor keeps logical focus. Likewise the task-press plan is built with BuildMarkdownCommand (2036) before the selection is committed and capture taken, so an exception there (bad\_alloc, a planner invalid\_argument) aborts ordinary caret placement for a click that merely landed on a checkbox.
- **Failure**: Embedded host: the application has not yet run its next Prepare after an edit; the user clicks the editor. Focus moves to the editor (TSF session re-targets), HitSource throws, OnMouseDown returns false, no caret placement, no capture; the host treats the gesture as unhandled although the focused control changed.
- **Fix**: Check `LayoutIsCurrent(host) && ! host.HasPendingContentPreparation()` before SetFocusControl and return false early (or return true after focusing without a caret change, consistently); commit selection and capture first, then build the task plan inside its own try so a planner failure only disables the checkbox gesture.
- **Verifier (, confirmed)**: Scenario A (either host): editor shows a prepared document; user types one character (Changed -> RequestContentPreparation sets host \_pendingContentPreparation) and clicks in the editor before the application's next Prepare / WM\_PAINT drains preparation (or any other control in the same host has requested content preparation, since the flag is host-wide). ## Semantic builder, display map and HTML subset (15)

### semantic-02

**Native object budget is enforced inconsistently across math, diagrams and images, so a document can be published that geometry must reject wholesale**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:551` · bug · major · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.5

- **What**: Three admission rules guard one shared 128-object budget. Math admits only while maths.size() + diagrams.size() + images.size() \< MaximumObjects (lines 329-330). Diagrams admit by a separate candidates counter (line 551, ++extensions.candidates > MaximumObjects) that ignores maths already admitted. Images admit while images.size() \< MaximumObjects (line 371) and are later re-capped by the image stage against diagrams+maths (DxUi.RichImageResources.h:86-87). Native geometry then enforces the combined contract strictly: nativeCount = diagrams + maths; if nativeCount > 128 it throws NativeObjectCapacity (DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:927-930), which the editor turns into nativeGeometryRejected and semanticDocument.reset() (DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp:1030-1048, 935-936, 948-949), i.e. [...]
- **Failure**: Document with 100 inline formulas '\$x\$' followed by 29 '''mermaid flowcharts: the builder admits all 100 maths (count \< 128) and all 29 diagrams (candidates \<= 128), publishes a SemanticDocument with 129 native objects; PreparedSemanticGeometry::BeginPreparation throws NativeObjectCapacity("Combined native image/diagram capacity exceeded"); the editor records the rejection and renders the whole document as raw Markdown source. The reverse order (29 diagrams then 100 formulas) correctly rejects only the 100th formula with a diagnostic. [...]
- **Fix**: Keep one admitted-object counter in SemanticExtensions (maths + published diagrams) and check it in AdvanceDiagram before emplace\_back (line 689-695) exactly as the math path does; drop images from the math check (placements are capped downstream against the same counter) or count placements, not elements. Add a test with maths before diagrams that asserts a DiagramCapacity diagnostic and a published semantic document rather than a geometry rejection.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: A RichMarkdown document with at least 128 valid inline formulas (for example '\$x\$') before one or more valid '''mermaid flowcharts: the builder admits 128 maths plus every diagram candidate. Geometry Begin then throws NativeObjectCapacity, and the editor shows the entire document as raw source instead of keeping the extra diagram as code with a DiagramCapacity diagnostic.
- **Existing tests**: None for combined math and diagram admission. Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.SemanticDiagrams.h:106-114 covers diagram-only capacity (129 diagrams give 128 published and one DiagramCapacity diagnostic).

### semantic-01

**Data-dependent builder invariant failures are rethrown as logic\_error/invalid\_argument, which the retained editor treats as transient: endless rebuild-and-fail loop**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:1128` · bug · minor · refuted (refute: refuted; reproduce: uncertain) · plan: not scheduled (refuted)

- **What**: SemanticBuilder::Advance catches every std::exception, discards the staged Preparation and rethrows unchanged. Several conditions that depend only on the input syntax are signalled with std::logic\_error or std::invalid\_argument rather than std::length\_error: ResolveSemanticValue throws logic\_error when the parser value cannot be reconstructed from source tokens (DxUi.RichSemanticValues.h:167), NativeHtmlPlan::At throws logic\_error on token ownership mismatch (DxUi.RichSemanticHtml.h:355), PrepareSourceFormats throws logic\_error "Ambiguous source format ownership" (line 1048), DiagramSource throws logic\_error (line 577), and SourceDisplayMap's constructor throws invalid\_argument for any span invariant violation (DxUi.RichSemantic.cpp:60-111). [...]
- **Failure**: Any RichMarkdown document for which one inline's reconstruction differs from the parser value (one Text node whose decoded units differ from node.value is enough) -> ResolveSemanticValue throws logic\_error at that inline -> Advance resets next\_ and rethrows -> editor cancels, invalidates layout, returns E\_FAIL -> host sets \_contentPreparationPending -> next pass rebuilds from scratch and fails again; the control shows no layout and repeats a full O(document) rebuild on every preparation pass until the user edits.
- **Fix**: Give the builder one typed rejection for data-dependent failures (e.g. a SemanticRejection exception derived from std::length\_error, or make Advance return a Rejected status that keeps Published()) so the editor records semanticRejected for that exact syntax and falls back to Source presentation once; keep logic\_error only for true programming errors. Add a test that injects a mismatch (e.g. a hand-built MarkdownSyntax with a wrong inline value) and asserts exactly one rejection and no re-preparation.
- **Verifier (refute, refuted)**: The mechanics are quoted correctly. SemanticBuilder::Advance (DxUi.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:1128-1132) resets next\_ and rethrows. RichTextEditor::PrepareContent records semanticRejected only for std::length\_error (RichTextEditor.cpp:922-929). The generic std::exception catch (1079-1087) cancels and returns E\_FAIL. This split between sticky and retryable failures is the written contract, not an oversight. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:175-209 (TestRichSemanticDocumentPinnedSourceCases) requires failures == 0 for all 1,324 pinned CommonMark/GFM cases, which exercises ResolveSemanticValue and SourceDisplayMap reconstruction. No RichMarkdown-profile corpus exists.

### semantic-03

**Rejected inline math overwrites the parent element's title, clobbering Markdown link titles exposed as UIA HelpText**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:344` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.5

- **What**: When ParseMath fails or the record/object budget is exhausted, the builder stores the explanation by assigning document->elements\_[visit.parent].title. visit.parent is whatever element encloses the inline: for math inside Markdown link text it is the Link element whose title already holds the link's own title attribute (set at line 357 from node.title), and for an HTML anchor it is the native Link element. The UIA Text provider publishes element.title (falling back to destination) as UIA\_HelpTextPropertyId (DxUi.RichAccessibility.h:257), so the link's HelpText is replaced by the math message and the link title is lost from the published document. Headings and table cells receive the message as their HelpText as well. [...]
- **Failure**: Source `[release notes $\badmacro{x}$] (https://example.invalid "Notes for v2")` -> ParseMath rejects \\badmacro -> line 344 sets the Link element's title to "Unsupported or oversized native equation. Exact source is preserved." -> UIA HelpText of the link reads the math message instead of "Notes for v2"; the title is also unavailable to any consumer of SemanticElement::title for that link.
- **Fix**: Do not mutate the parent. Either create a Math-role element for the rejected formula (display = the literal span) and put the message in its title, or leave the explanation to Diagnostics() and let the accessibility layer derive HelpText from the diagnostic for that element. Add a test with math inside a titled link asserting the link title survives.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: RichMarkdown source `[release notes $\badmacro{x}$] (https://example.invalid "Notes for v2")`: the Link element's title becomes 'Unsupported or oversized native equation. Exact source is preserved.', and UIA HelpText for the link reports that message instead of 'Notes for v2'. A rejected formula inside a native HTML \<a> overwrites the enclosing paragraph's title, not the anchor's.
- **Existing tests**: None. No test places a rejected formula inside a titled link.

### semantic-04

**A leaf whose display already ends with a transformed LF (\<br>) skips the generated cell/paragraph separator**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:845` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: On block close the builder emits a Generated "\\t" (non-last table column) or "\\n" anchored at content.last only if output.text does not already end with '\\n'. A native \<br> emits a Transformed "\\n" owned by the tag source (line 270), so a cell or paragraph that ends with \<br> contributes no generated separator: the tab that separates table columns in the flat display text is dropped and the paragraph has no generated EOL caret site at content.last; the trailing display LF is instead owned by the \<br> source span.
- **Failure**: GFM table `| a<br> | b |` -> cell 0 display is "a\\n" (Transformed from `<br>`), close sees output.text.back() == '\\n' and emits no "\\t"; the flat display for the row becomes "a\\nb\\n" instead of "a\\n\\tb\\n", so any consumer of Map().Display() (non-native fallback layout, display-text element deletion ranges) sees the two cells joined.
- **Fix**: Track whether the leaf's own content emitted the final LF as a Transformed object versus a Generated separator; for table cells always emit the column separator, and for paragraphs emit the generated EOL unless the last span is a Generated EOL. Add a `| a<br> | b |` fixture to the semantic HTML tests.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Only GFM/RichMarkdown table cells are affected (for example a header row, a delimiter row, then `| a<br> | b |`). The cell ending in \<br> gets no generated '\\t', so the flat display is "a\\nb\\n" rather than "a\\n\\tb\\n". Backspace at the start of the following cell then reaches the \<br> LF and deletes `<br>` from the previous cell, whereas a normal cell boundary is inert. Paragraphs ending in \<br> are intentionally unchanged.
- **Existing tests**: Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.h:194 and :281 pin the paragraph behavior (no extra generated EOL after a trailing \<br>) as intended. :121 pins tab separators for ordinary cells. No test covers a table cell ending with \<br>.

### semantic-05

**ApplySourceEdits rejects an insertion followed by a replacement at the same offset, beyond the documented 'two insertions' rule**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemantic.cpp:243` · doc-mismatch · minor · disputed (refute: refuted; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: The ordering check rejects any edit whose first equals the previous edit's first. The header (include/DxUi/RichSemantic.h:68-69) documents that touching edits are legal and only two insertions at the same boundary are ambiguous. An insertion [X,X) followed by a replacement [X,Y) is well ordered (insertion text, then replacement) yet rejected, while the mirror case replacement [W,X) followed by insertion [X,X) is accepted. Planners must therefore merge such pairs themselves.
- **Failure**: edits = { {{5,5}, L"a"}, {{5,7}, L"b"} } on a current snapshot -> throws std::invalid\_argument("Semantic edits must be ordered and disjoint") although the result "a"+"b" replacing [5,7) is unambiguous; edits = { {{3,5}, L"a"}, {{5,5}, L"b"} } is accepted.
- **Fix**: Either tighten the check to `edit.source.first == previousStart && edit.source.last == edit.source.first && previousEnd == previousStart` (reject only insertion/insertion) or update the header and docs to state that an edit may not start where the previous edit started.
- **Verifier (refute, refuted)**: The code does exactly what the normative contract says. Specs/UI/UI\_ControlsAndLayout.md:1070-1071 reads: "Edits use ascending disjoint source ranges; touching ranges are allowed but two edits with the same start are rejected." The check at DxUi.RichSemantic.cpp:243 (`edit.source.first < previousEnd || edit.source.first == previousStart`) implements that rule. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichSemantic.h:204 rejects two insertions at the same boundary; :178-180 accepts touching edits. No test accepts insertion+replacement at the same start, and the spec forbids it.

`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:213` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: uncertain; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13, W4.2

After: reserve name for roles whose accessible name differs from display (Image alternative, Diagram alternative, Footnote label, FootnoteReference/Backlink labels) and let the accessibility fallback serve text roles.

The copies are redundant for the internal UIA reader but form part of the public SemanticElement data that tests pin for Code. A change needs an API decision (for example, documenting that name is set only when it differs from display) and test updates, not a silent removal.
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.SemanticSourceBlock.h:99 and :263 (Code element.name equals the raw display); Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichTextAccessibility.cpp:1933 (looks up UIA part by Elements()[i].name).

This is documented, intended preparation design, and its large-document cost is the already-planned D4c/M6c work rather than a new defect. - **Existing tests**: None specific to per-keystroke map allocation. Large-document latency is explicitly unqualified (M6c and D4c HOLD).

### semantic-12

**Inline math parses the full expression before any admission check, re-walks the tree to count records, and its diagnostics bypass the capacity guard used for diagrams**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:319` · simplification · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.5

- **What**: For every Math inline the builder calls ParseMath (allocating up to 512 nodes) and only then checks the 4096 total-record and 128 object budgets; the record count is obtained by a recursive lambda walk that ParseMath could return. When the budget is exhausted the parse work is discarded. The failure path pushes into extensions.diagnostics without the MaximumElements check that DiagramDiagnostic applies (line 534-535), so the two extension diagnostics paths have different capacity policies (bounded in practice by the inline count, so not an overflow today).
- **Failure**: Before: a document with 200 `$...$` formulas after the 128-object budget is full still runs 72 full ParseMath calls whose results are thrown away, plus a tree walk each. After: check object/record budgets first (records can be bounded by MathMaximumSourceUnits or returned by ParseMath), parse only admissible candidates, and route math diagnostics through the same guarded helper as diagrams.
- **Fix**: Have ParseMath report node count in MathExpression, check object capacity before parsing, and share one ExtensionDiagnostic(visit, kind, source, message) helper for math and diagrams.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: A RichMarkdown document with more than 128 native objects, for example 200 short `$x^2$` formulas, runs ParseMath, a shared\_ptr allocation and a tree walk for each of the 72 formulas past the budget, then discards the results and emits the same UnsupportedMath fallback. Total wasted work is O(source), bounded by MathMaximumSourceUnits and 512 nodes per formula. Output is correct.
- **Existing tests**: None found: no test references UnsupportedMath or the 128-object/4,096-record math budget path.

ParseNativeHtmlTag only recognises element tags, so a comment `<!-- ... -->` (block type 2 or inline) makes the paragraph or block literal: the whole paragraph loses its Markdown styling and the comment text itself is displayed in code ink. This is the documented policy (docs/rich-text.md:979-982, UI\_ControlsAndLayout.md:1184-1189), so it is not a defect, but comments are among the most common raw-HTML constructs in Markdown (`<!-- TOC -->`, hidden editorial notes) and every other renderer hides them. After: the comment becomes a hidden (zero-display, source-owned) span, the paragraph keeps bold rendering, and the comment stays editable source (caret affinities via the existing hidden-source rules).
- **Fix**: Add a `Comment` NativeHtmlKind (and optionally processing instructions/declarations) that validates as a self-contained token with no display text, participates in no stack, and is hidden in both inline and block plans; document it in the HTML subset table and add fixtures. Keep unknown element tags on the literal path.
- **Verifier (refute, refuted)**: The trace is accurate. MarkdownInlines.h:878-880 turns `<!-- ... -->` into an Html inline node, and MarkdownBlocks.h:385 turns it into an HTML block. ParseNativeHtmlTag (RichSemanticHtml.h:124-129) gets an empty name after '\<', so it finds no match and returns false. NativeHtmlPlan::Inline has already set present=true (line 288) and returns at line 291, and Block returns at line 345. LeafBlock (RichSemanticDocument.cpp:788-795) then emits the leaf as a literal with Code. [...]
- **Existing tests**: No semantic test pins a comment specifically. Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.h:211-215 pins the same whole-leaf literal policy for \<script>, a span with a class attribute, nested block wrappers and a data: anchor. [...]

### semantic-15

**Native anchor title attribute is decoded but not filtered, so control characters reach UIA HelpText**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticHtml.h:175` · security · minor · refuted (refute: refuted; reproduce: refuted) · plan: not scheduled (refuted)

- **What**: ParseNativeHtmlTag applies NativeHtmlDestination's C0/C1/space/quote policy to href only; the title attribute is stored after DecodeHtmlAttribute with no filtering. The decoded title becomes SemanticElement::title (DxUi.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:263) and is published verbatim as UIA\_HelpTextPropertyId (DxUi.RichAccessibility.h:257). Markdown link titles pass through the parser's own decoder with the same gap, so this is a policy consistency issue rather than an exploit: nothing executes, but assistive technology receives raw control characters.
- **Failure**: `<a href="#x" title="ok&#1;&#10;&#13;hidden">t</a>` validates as native; the Link element's title contains U+0001, LF and CR, and a screen reader's HelpText announcement includes those controls.
- **Fix**: Apply a shared text-attribute policy (reject or replace C0/C1 controls other than TAB, collapse CR/LF to space) to native titles and, for consistency, to Markdown link/image titles when they are published as UIA text; document it next to the href rules.
- **Verifier (refute, refuted)**: The mechanics are right. DecodeHtmlAttribute decodes `&#1;&#10;&#13;` into U+0001, LF and CR (AppendScalar in MarkdownSyntax.cpp:55-58 replaces only 0, surrogates and >0x10FFFF). ParseNativeHtmlTag stores the result as tag.title (RichSemanticHtml.h:173-177), the builder copies it to element.title (RichSemanticDocument.cpp:263), and RichAccessibility.h:257 publishes it as HelpText. That behavior matches the written contract and the rest of the library, so it is not a defect. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.h:190,196,207 pin title acceptance and reference decoding (`title='A &quot;B&quot;'`). No test pins the handling of control characters in titles.

## Semantic geometry (15)

### geometry-01

**StepSource walks boxes in display order, so reordered footnote bodies are unreachable by Left/Right and Right dead-ends before a visible footer**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:1447` · bug · major · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.10

- **What**: After the in-box cluster walk, StepSource searches neighbours with `while (forwards ? neighbor + 1u < state.boxes.size() : neighbor > 0u)` over `state.boxes`, which is layout (display) order, and accepts a neighbour only when `forwards ? raw > source : raw < source` (line 1463) or, for caret boxes, `insertion >= source` (line 1457). Footnote definitions are displayed after all main text but their source lies in the middle, so forward traversal from main text either jumps over the definition's source (a later main-text box already satisfies `raw > source`) or, from the last main box, finds only footer boxes whose `raw` is smaller and returns `{source, index}` unchanged (line 1477). Left from the definition start does go back to main text, so traversal is asymmetric. [...]
- **Failure**: Source `A[^1]\n\n[^1]: note body\n\nB`. (1) Caret at the end of `B`, press Right: the cluster walk ends at B's display.last, the only later boxes are the footer boxes whose `ToSource(display.first)` is inside the definition (\< caret source), the inner loop never produces `raw > source`, so the caret stays put although `1. note body` is visibly below. (2) Caret on the blank line before the definition, press Right: the next qualifying neighbour is the caret box of the blank line after the definition (`insertion >= source`), so the whole definition body is skipped; [...]
- **Fix**: When the neighbour walk is forward and no later layout-order box qualifies (or when a later box skips over an unvisited source interval), fall back to the source-ordered `sourceBoxes` index already built in Phase::IndexSourceBoxes: pick the box whose SourceFirst is the smallest value greater than `source` (or largest smaller for backwards) and step into it. Add geometry tests for Right from the last main paragraph into the footer, Right/Left across the definition body, and Right at the end of the footer.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Source `A[^1]\n\n[^1]: note body\n\nB` (RichMarkdown, footnotes enabled). (a) Put the caret on the blank line at source 6 and press Right: the caret jumps to 24 (start of B), skipping the definition source 7..23. Left from 24 jumps back to 6, so Left/Right can never enter the moved body from main text. (b) Put the caret at the end of `note body` and press Right: it moves to the footer blank site at 23, then stays at 23 on every later Right, although B (24..25) has larger source offsets. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Scope.Tests.RichSemanticGeometry.h:604-614 checks only monotonic StepSource on non-footnote sources. ReorderedFootnoteCaretSitesAndSourceLines (lines 32-132) checks SourceCaret, HitTestSource and SourceLines but never StepSource. Scope.Tests.Footnotes.h:220-232 covers Shift+Down only. [...]

### geometry-02

**Draw walks every element and every box per call and re-submits whole paragraph layouts per band; per-selection-line calls make this quadratic**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:1675` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.7

- **What**: `Draw` has no spatial index: `for (std::size_t i = 0u; i < elements.size(); ++i)` scans up to 32,768 element bounds, then `for (const auto& box : state.boxes)` scans up to 4,096 boxes, and for every box whose frame intersects `[top,bottom)` it calls `box->layout.Draw(...)` which submits the complete paragraph layout (line 1709). The band interface forces the editor (DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp:1417-1426) to call Draw once per visible selection rectangle, i.e. once per visual line, so a selected wrapped paragraph is rasterized once per selected line. v0 bounds the same pattern by 1 Ki-unit chunks and 128 chunks (RichDocumentLayout.cpp:656-660); semantic boxes are whole paragraphs up to the 64 Ki source cap.
- **Failure**: A 20,000-unit paragraph wrapped to ~400 lines with 60 lines visible and selected: each paint runs 1 base pass plus 60 selection passes, each pass scanning 32,768 element bounds and 4,096 box frames and issuing a full 400-line DrawTextLayout clipped to a one-line band (~24,000 line submissions per frame instead of ~460). The same per-call element scan also runs for documents with no decoration at all.
- **Fix**: Keep a y-sorted box index and a y-sorted decorated-element index built during Phase::ParentBounds (both already bounded), binary-search the first candidate for `top`, and stop at the first frame whose top >= bottom. Offer a Draw overload that takes the list of selection rectangles and draws each intersecting layout once with a layer/clip geometry, or draw selected text via per-run brush ranges instead of re-submitting the paragraph per band.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Take a semantic document with one long paragraph built from many source lines or soft wraps (for example about 400 visual lines), with 60 visible lines selected. Each paint makes 1 + 60 Draw calls. Each call rescans every element bound and box frame and re-submits the full 400-line DrawTextLayout clipped to a one-line band, about 24k line/glyph-run submissions per frame instead of about 460. The cost is bounded by the paragraph/element caps and the viewport line count. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None measures paint cost with a selection. The plan's '1,000 warm composites' figures are unpaired diagnostics without large selections, and D4c paired measurement is still on HOLD.

### geometry-03

**A single diagram wider than the view rejects native geometry for the whole document (source fallback) instead of a per-object literal fallback**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:749` · architecture · minor · disputed (refute: refuted; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C6

- **What**: `if (extent.width > options.widthDip) throw NativeObjectCapacity("Diagram does not fit this view width");` (749-750) and `if (textBounds.second > options.widthDip + 0.01f) throw NativeObjectCapacity("Diagram and its marker do not fit this view width")` (834-835) abort the entire preparation. The editor maps NativeObjectCapacity to `nativeGeometryRejected` and presents the complete document as exact source (DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp:1030-1049). docs/rich-text.md:36-37 documents this ("Width/font rejection keeps usable exact source and retries after a matching view/resource change"), so it is intended, but it is the only geometry condition where one object degrades every other paragraph, table, image and formula; [...]
- **Failure**: A document with 200 rendered paragraphs, tables and images plus one `flowchart LR` whose routed extent is 900 DIP: shrinking the window to 800 DIP wide flips the whole document to raw Markdown, every semantic UIA child is retired (`RetireSemanticAccessibility()`), caret sites are lost, and widening the window flips it all back. The same applies to a diagram inside a list item or table cell whose `options.widthDip` is the (narrower) cell width.
- **Fix**: Treat an over-wide diagram like unsupported syntax: null the placement for that element, present its fence as literal source with a DiagramCapacity-style diagnostic, and continue preparing the rest of the document; alternatively scale the paint plan down to the available width (PreparedFlowchart::Draw already takes an origin; a uniform scale like PreparedMath::Draw uses would preserve hit testing by scaling bounds). Update docs/rich-text.md:36-37 accordingly.
- **Verifier (refute, refuted)**: The mechanism is described accurately but is the contracted, tested behavior, so it is not a defect. NativeObjectCapacity is thrown at RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:749-750 and 834-835. The editor records `nativeGeometryRejected` (RichTextEditor.cpp:1030-1049), drops the semantic document for that exact key (935-936) and forces ViewMode::Source for the whole document (948-949). The normative contract makes this intended. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.SemanticDiagrams.h:276-314 (Editor) pins whole-document SourceFallback on a narrow view and Semantic recovery on a wider one.

### geometry-04

**Draw culls boxes by logical frame and ignores glyph overhang; DrawingVerticalBounds is never consulted by the semantic path**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:1692` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.10

- **What**: `box->frame` is built from `box->layout.Height()` (line 845-846), i.e. DWRITE\_TEXT\_METRICS.height, and Draw keeps only boxes with `box->frame.bottom > top && box->frame.top < bottom` (1692; same test for images at 1719). PreparedLayout already computes overhang-inclusive `drawingTop/drawingBottom` (RichTextLayout.cpp:296-297, exposed by `DrawingVerticalBounds()` at 353) and v0 uses them plus a 2-pixel DPI-aware pad before culling (RichDocumentLayout.cpp:631-638, 658). The semantic path uses neither, so ink that extends outside the logical line box is dropped at the viewport edges.
- **Failure**: Scroll so that a paragraph's logical frame.bottom equals `scrollDip` exactly while its last line ends with a glyph with bottom overhang (a tall emoji, a combining mark below, or an italic descender). `frame.bottom > top` is false, the layout is not drawn, and the overhanging pixels that lie inside the viewport are missing until the next scroll moves the frame into the band. The same clip happens at the top edge for above-line overhang (accents on capital letters).
- **Fix**: Store `layout.DrawingVerticalBounds()` per box (plus the marker/native extents) as a separate paint rectangle and use it for Draw culling with the same 2-pixel/DPI pad as v0; keep `frame` for hit testing and visible-range logic.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Use a paragraph whose last line has glyph ink below the DWrite line box, for example several stacked combining marks below a base letter. Scroll so that frame.bottom \<= scrollDip \< frame.bottom + overhang.bottom. Draw culls the box and the overhanging ink inside the top of the viewport is not painted. Scrolling a little further up repaints it. The same happens at the viewport bottom for ink above frame.top. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None. No semantic raster test covers edge-of-viewport overhang.

### geometry-05

**Math placements are forced through NativeImageGeometry, coupling DWrite-only formulas to a D2D render target identity**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:289` · architecture · minor · confirmed (feasibility: confirmed) · plan: W2.5

- **What**: `State` creates `NativeImageGeometry` when `!SemanticExtensionAccess::Maths(*document).empty()` even with no images (289-293). Its constructor throws `std::invalid_argument("Image raster preparation requires the owner's target")` when `target` is null (117-118), `Preparation` retains the target as `raster(state->images ? target : nullptr)` (412, 424) across staging, `Begin` keys reuse on `state.images->targetIdentity == raster` (938), and `Draw` rejects any other target (1670-1671). PreparedMath only creates IDWriteTextLayouts (DxUi.PreparedMath.h:318-327) and draws with the caller's brush, so none of this is needed for formulas. `NativeImagePlacement` carries both `pixels` and `math` with `Size()/Baseline()` switching on which is set (99-106), and Draw branches again at 1721.
- **Failure**: A caller (test harness, headless measurement, or a host that prepares before its first device exists) calls `PreparedSemanticGeometry::Begin(factory, document, options)` for a document containing `$x^2$` and no images: preparation throws invalid\_argument although nothing in the formula pipeline touches Direct2D. After device replacement a math-only document is treated as target-bound and must be fully re-laid out even when only the raster target changed.
- **Fix**: Split formulas into their own `NativeMathGeometry` (element, display, PreparedMath, bounds, owner) with no target, make `targetIdentity`/`raster` retention conditional on real image placements, and let `SemanticBox` hold a small span of inline-object records (image or math) instead of scanning `images->placements` for `owner == this` in Caret/Range/Draw.
- **Verifier (feasibility, confirmed)**: There is no production failure, because the editor always supplies the host context. Only a direct caller of the Detail API is affected. For example, PreparedSemanticGeometry::Begin(factory, doc, options) on a document whose only native object is `$x^2$` throws invalid\_argument at line 118, although formulas need no Direct2D resource. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticEditor.h:254-287 (MathAndBroaderDiagrams) and Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.NativeDevicePolicy.h:33 cover math only through the host, with a real context. No test prepares a math document with a null raster.

`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:605` · simplification · minor · confirmed (feasibility: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

'PreparedSemanticGeometry::Advance' (953-1315) is a single 'while' with a 15-branch 'if/else if' chain, each phase inlined, and 'boundsIndex' is reused as the cursor for six different phases (968/976, 995/1001, 1019/1026, 1033/1041, 1272/1277, 1286/1291) and reset to zero at each transition, alongside 'paragraph, rowStart, rowPlacement, rowTable, rowIndex, y, rowHeight, rowActive, emptyLine, plannedBoxes, markerIndex, l [...]

After: `Advance` dispatches to one member function per phase (`IndexElementsStep()`, `SourceLinesStep()`, ...) each owning its own cursor, and `LayoutOne` is split into `NextItem()`, `PlaceCell()`, `AttachInlineObjects()` and `AlignMarker()`.
- **Fix**: Extract one method per Phase and one helper per LayoutOne responsibility; give each phase its own cursor field (or a `std::variant` of per-phase state) so a cursor can never leak between phases.
- **Verifier (feasibility, confirmed)**: Before: inserting a new phase that uses boundsIndex anywhere between CountSites and InlineBounds, or forgetting to reset it, silently starts InlineBounds partway through the element array. InlineBounds depends on a zero set at 970, 997 or 1037. ParentBounds likewise depends on InlineBounds ending at elements.size(). After: have Advance call one member function per phase. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticGeometry.h:27, 52, 163 and 595 pin the per-turn blockSteps and layoutsPrepared budgets, and line 601 pins that admission is rejected before any SDK call. All of these must keep passing unchanged after a refactor.

### geometry-07

**The paragraph/empty-line merge decision is duplicated between CountSites preflight and LayoutOne**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:1049` · simplification · minor · confirmed (feasibility: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: Phase::CountSites (1049-1082) re-implements the merge performed by LayoutOne (616-676): the LiteralDefinitionLine skip, the `paragraph == paragraphs.size() || EmptyBefore(...)` choice, `EmptyOwnsParagraph` ownership, the `SplitMarker` +1 and the cursor increments. Any future change to one (for example the definitions-only EOF box synthesised at 634-640, which the preflight does not count and is correct only because it occurs when `boxes` is empty) must be mirrored by hand or admission and layout disagree.

After: one `MergeStep NextItem(cursor)` returns {consumeParagraph, consumeEmptyLine, caretLine, boxes} and both phases call it, so admission counts exactly what layout creates.

Either way it breaks the rule that admission is enforced before allocation and SDK work. After: a pure `NextItem(paragraph, emptyLine)` returns {consumeParagraph, consumeEmptyLine, caretOnly, boxCount}. CountSites adds up boxCount. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticGeometry.h:588-603 (an overflow of 4,097 blank lines is rejected with zero SDK calls and the prior publication preserved) and 584 (an unclosed code body produces exactly one box). Both would guard the refactor.

### geometry-10

**SourceBox recovers a box index by pointer scan after BoxAt already located it**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:359` · simplification · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: `BoxAt` returns `const SemanticBox&` found via `glyphBoxes` binary search (328-334). `SourceBox` then does `const auto* chosen = &BoxAt(...); const auto found = std::find_if(boxes.begin(), boxes.end(), [&] (const auto& box) { return box.get() == chosen; }); return found - boxes.begin();` (359-361), a linear scan over up to 4,096 unique\_ptrs to recover an index the search already had (`*found` in glyphBoxes is the box index). SourceBox is on the paint path (SourceCaret at 1390 every frame when the site affinity misses) and on every Erase/Step/Home/End query.
- **Failure**: Before: every SourceCaret call whose `site` is MarkdownNoNode (e.g. after SetSelection, composition, or structural commands) costs one O(boxes) caret-box scan (352-358) plus one O(boxes) pointer scan. After: `std::size_t BoxIndexAt(position)` returns `*found` directly; `BoxAt` becomes `*boxes[BoxIndexAt(position)]`.
- **Fix**: Add `BoxIndexAt` returning the glyph-box index and use it in SourceBox; optionally store `index` in SemanticBox when pushing.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: When SourceCaret, StepSource, SourceLineBoundary or the site predicates are given a site that misses, and no source-caret box owns the offset, SourceBox does a second O(boxes) pointer scan to recover an index the glyphBoxes binary search already had. This is redundant CPU work and code, not a correctness problem. The asymptotic cost is unchanged because the preceding caret-box loop is already O(boxes).
- **Existing tests**: None pins the pointer-scan form. Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticGeometry.h exercises SourceCaret/StepSource through the fallback, e.g. StepSource(raw, MarkdownNoNode, ...) at lines 609-613.

That sets lastLayoutCalls = 1 against the default 4-layout owner budget (LayoutWorkBudget::maximumLayouts = 4, RichTextLayout.h:134). CountSites also counts each empty line as one planned box against MaximumParagraphs = 4096 (1063-1082). Both outcomes are documented and tested as intended, however. [...]

After: either the v0 path and the semantic path share the source-based API (RichEditorLayout exposes only SourceCaret/HitTestSource/StepSource/SourceLineBoundary and adapts v0 to them), or the display methods are removed from PreparedSemanticGeometry and the tests target the source methods.

### geometry-13

**Equal-width cells have no minimum width: narrow views wrap every glyph and push cell text outside its clipped frame**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:725` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.10

- **What**: `cellWidth = box->width / columns` (725), `box->x += cellWidth * column + 6.0f` (726), `box->width = std::max(1.0f, cellWidth - 12.0f)` (728). When `cellWidth < 12` the layout width becomes 1 DIP so DirectWrite places one glyph per line, the row height grows with every character, and the text origin sits 6 DIP right of a frame that is narrower than 6 DIP, while Draw clips to that frame (1700-1703) and Range clips rectangles to it (1560-1566). Table commands admit 64 columns (docs/rich-text.md:1063), so this occurs for any 64-column table below 768 DIP plus indent, and earlier for indented tables.
- **Failure**: A 40-column GFM table in a 400 DIP viewport: cellWidth = 10 DIP, every cell layout is 1 DIP wide, a 12-character cell becomes 12 lines, the row becomes ~250 DIP tall, and all glyphs (drawn at x >= cell.left + 6 with advance > 4) are clipped away so the row is a tall blank grid with no caret-visible text and no diagnostic; the document is still reported Semantic.
- **Fix**: Define a minimum cell width (e.g. 4 \* fontSizeDip); when `columns * minimum > box->width`, either lay the table out at its minimum width and allow horizontal overflow/clipping at the table level with a diagnostic, or present that table as literal source like other unsupported presentation; add a test for 64 columns at narrow widths in LTR and RTL.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: A 40-column GFM table in a 400 DIP Rich view (no indent): cellWidth = 10 DIP, every cell DirectWrite layout is 1 DIP wide with WRAP, so a 12-character cell becomes 12 lines and the row grows to ~12 line heights + 12 DIP. In LTR, each glyph is drawn from frame.left+6 inside a 10-DIP axis-aligned clip, so only a ~4 DIP sliver of each character is visible. At 64 columns below ~384 DIP (cellWidth \<= 6) the LTR text is clipped away entirely. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None for narrow tables. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticGeometry.h:286-290 covers only 1-DIP list gutters (finite caret). Scope.Tests.RichSemanticGeometry.h:293-299 lays out a 2-column nested table at 300 DIP.

### geometry-14

**Hit testing and visible-range queries scan all boxes linearly although boxes are laid out in nondecreasing y**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:369` · performance · minor · refuted (refute: refuted; reproduce: refuted) · plan: not scheduled (refuted)

- **What**: `NearestBox` loops `for (std::size_t i = 0u; i < boxes.size(); ++i)` computing a distance for every box (369-380); `VisibleSourceRange` (1523), `VisibleRange` (1648), `SourceSiteForSyntaxNode` (1511) and the caret-box scan in `SourceBox` (352) are also O(boxes). NearestBox runs on every pointer move during drag selection (HitSource at DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp:1479) and twice per vertical arrow key (1544, 1548). v0 uses `std::upper_bound` on chunk y (RichDocumentLayout.cpp:532). Boxes are emitted with nondecreasing `y` except within one table row and the marker/body pair, so a y-sorted index with a per-row group would make these logarithmic.

It is called from HitSource (RichTextEditor.cpp:1479) on each drag move (2106) and from vertical arrow navigation (1544/1548). [...]

### geometry-15

**VisibleRange/VisibleSourceRange extend the result to a box start even when none of its lines is visible**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:1533` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.10

- **What**: For every box whose frame intersects the band, `const auto range = box->layout.VisibleRange(top - box->y, bottom - box->y); first = min(first, ToSource(display.first + range.first)); last = max(last, ToSource(display.first + range.second, Trailing))` (1533-1535; same at 1651-1653). `PreparedLayout::VisibleRange` returns `{0, 0}` when no line intersects (RichTextLayout.cpp:464-478). Table cell frames include the 6 DIP top padding (box->y = row top + 6, frame.top = row top via PlaceRowOne 905), so a band ending inside that padding intersects the frame while no line is visible, and the result is extended to the cell's start. v0 guards exactly this: "Only {0,0} for a nonempty layout means no line here" (RichDocumentLayout.cpp:614-617).
- **Failure**: Viewport bottom lands 3 DIP into the top padding of a table row: `frame.top < bottom` is true, `layout.VisibleRange(top', bottom')` with bottom' \< 0 returns {0,0}, and GetVisibleSourceRange reports `last` at the first cell's source start although nothing of that row is on screen; UIA/TSF visible-range consumers receive a range that includes an invisible paragraph boundary.
- **Fix**: Skip a box when `range.first == range.second && range.first != layout text size` (mirror v0), or test visibility against the box's text extent (`box->y .. box->y + box->height`) instead of the padded frame.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: The viewport bottom lands 1-6 DIP below the top of a table row: every cell frame intersects, each cell's PreparedLayout::VisibleRange returns {0, 0}, and GetVisibleSourceRange/UIA GetVisibleRanges reports last at the source start of the row's last cell (or the end of a padded cell's caret source). The reported range thus includes all text of the preceding cells in that row, none of which is on screen. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Scope.Tests.RichTextInput.cpp:636-637 asserts that the visible source range excludes unshown wrapped lines (no table case). Scope.Tests.RichTextAccessibility.cpp:251-260 asserts that GetVisibleRanges excludes scrolled-off lines. Neither covers table padding.

## Math parser and recognizer (18)

### math-parser-01

**Parser accepts text leaves DirectWrite cannot lay out on one line; the resulting std::length\_error rejects the whole document's semantic preview**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:97` · bug · major · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.2

- **What**: ParseMath is the documented all-or-nothing gate (RichMath.h:60), but GroupText copies every unit into a Text leaf, including '\\n' (`else value += c;` at lines 97-98), and Row merges adjacent Text atoms into one run without any length bound (lines 413-414). PreparedMath::Advance then shapes each leaf with CreateTextLayout(..., 65536, 65536) and throws plain `std::length_error("Mathematical text leaf must be one line")` when GetLineMetrics reports more than one line (DxUi.PreparedMath.h:330-334), and `std::length_error("Mathematical geometry capacity exceeded")` at 339-340. [...]
- **Failure**: A RichMarkdown paragraph containing display math whose \\text{} spans a logical line, e.g. "\$\$\\text{first line" + newline + "second line}\$\$" (display delimiters may span lines per docs/rich-text.md:1221). Scan accepts it (display=true ignores '\\n'), ParseMath accepts it (Space() only skips whitespace outside groups; GroupText copies the '\\n'), the semantic builder admits a SemanticRole::Math element, then PreparedMath::Advance lays out "first line\\nsecond line", GetLineMetrics returns count 2, std::length\_error propagates out of PreparedSemanticGeometry::Advance (rethrown at DxUi.RichSemanticGe [...]
- **Fix**: Decide the all-or-nothing outcome in the parser: reject '\\n', '\\r', '\\t' and other control units inside GroupText and Atom text leaves, and cap a merged Text run (e.g. 256 units) in Row. In PreparedMath::Advance create the leaf layout with DWRITE\_WORD\_WRAPPING\_NO\_WRAP and throw NativeObjectCapacity (or better, mark the single placement as failed and let the geometry emit the source fallback for that formula only) instead of a bare std::length\_error that the editor treats as whole-syntax rejection. [...]
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: RichMarkdown paragraph "\$\$\\\\text{first line" CRLF "second line}\$\$". The Math value is "\\\\text{first line\\nsecond line}" and ParseMath accepts it as one Text leaf. PreparedMath::Advance gets 2 lines from DirectWrite and throws std::length\_error("Mathematical text leaf must be one line"). RichTextEditor::PrepareContent's std::length\_error handler sets semanticRejected and clears the semantic document, so every paragraph renders as exact source (SourceFallback) and the formula gets no UnsupportedMa [...]
- **Existing tests**: None. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:66-77 only checks single-line formulas such as \\\\text{speed in m/s}. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticEditor.h:254-287 uses only single-line math. No test covers a newline inside a \\\\text/\\\\operatorname group, or a leaf whose layout produces more than one line.

### math-parser-03

**Closer search is a raw scan that does not respect code spans or raw HTML between the opener and closer**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.MarkdownInlines.h:1218` · bug · major · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: The math branch runs first in Scan() and looks for the closing delimiter by scanning `text_` unit by unit (lines 1218-1236) without consulting `backtickNext_` (the precomputed code-span map used at line 1254) or HtmlEnd(). A '\$' that is not actually an opener (currency, or a stray dollar) therefore acquires everything up to the next qualifying '\$' even when that '\$' lives inside a code span or an HTML tag that CommonMark binds more tightly. docs/rich-text.md:1219-1220 promises that 'code spans ... retain their existing grammar' and the target envelope says 'Code spans, fenced non-math code, opaque HTML and link destinations do not acquire math'; that only holds when the code span starts before the dollar.
- **Failure**: RichMarkdown source 'Total \$5 and `$x` plus \$y\$'. At the first '\$', content is '5' (nonspace), the scan finds the '\$' inside the code span (preceded by '`', not space; followed by 'x', not a digit), so Append(Kind::Math, ..., "5 and `") is produced; ParseMath("5 and `") succeeds (all units become one merged Text leaf '5and`'), so the document renders a formula '5and`', the code span is destroyed and 'x` plus ' becomes plain text. Likewise 'a \$b \<i>c\$\</i>' makes the formula 'b\<i>c' and leaves a dangling '\</i>'.
- **Fix**: While scanning for the closer, when 'text\_[end] == L'''' and 'backtickNext\_[end] != MarkdownNoNode', jump 'end' past that code span (and treat a complete HTML tag via HtmlEnd() the same way) so a closer inside opaque content never matches; if the opener's scan crosses a code span and finds no closer, the dollar stays literal exactly as today. Extract the branch into a ScanMath() helper (Scan() is already ~157 lines) and add regression tests for '\$5 and '\$x'' and '\$a \<b>\$'.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: RichMarkdown 'It costs \$5; set `$PATH` first' (or 'Total \$5 and `$x` plus \$y\$'). The single-dollar scan closes on the '\$' inside the code span, so the formula '5;set`' (or '5and`') renders, the code span is lost, and the rest shows as plain text. The source stays exact.
- **Existing tests**: Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:51 ('`$literal$` and \\\\\$escaped and \$5 and \$10.') covers only a code span that opens before any dollar, plus currency without a qualifying closer. Nothing covers a currency dollar followed by a code span or HTML tag that contains '\$'.

### math-parser-02

**\\(...\\) inline math wrongly inherits the single-dollar currency heuristics, so '\\( x+1 \\)' is silently reduced to escaped text**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.MarkdownInlines.h:1215` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: The nonspace-content-edge and not-before-digit rules exist only to disambiguate '\$' from currency (docs/rich-text.md:1218-1219: 'Single-dollar math is single-line and requires nonspace content edges; a closing dollar followed by a digit is currency text'; DxUiMath1-target.json envelopes.inlineExplicit: 'Backslash-parenthesis pair; no physical newline' with no edge rule). The scanner keys both rules on `display`, which is false for `\(` (line 1212: `display = width == 2u && (dollars || text_[first + 1u] == L'[')`), so `\(` requires a nonspace first unit (line 1215 `(display || ! SourceSpace(text_[content]))`) and a nonspace unit before `\)` plus no digit after it (line 1231). [...]
- **Failure**: RichMarkdown source 'Area \\\\( x + 1 \\\\) units' (the conventional spaced LaTeX form). content points at ' ', so the math branch is skipped; the escape branch turns '\\\\(' into '(' and '\\\\)' into ')'. Output text is 'Area ( x + 1 ) units' with no Math element and no UnsupportedMath diagnostic; the same text with dollars '\$x + 1\$' would have rendered. Also '\\\\(n\\\\)2' (formula followed by a digit) is dropped by the digit rule, which has no currency meaning for backslash delimiters.
- **Fix**: Introduce `const bool currencyRules = dollars && width == 1u;` and use it instead of `! display` for the edge/digit checks, keeping only the no-newline restriction for `\(`. Add tests for '\\\\( x \\\\)' and '\\\\(n\\\\)2' producing Math elements, and update docs/rich-text.md to state the rule applies to single dollars only.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: RichMarkdown 'Area \\\\( x + 1 \\\\) units' displays 'Area ( x + 1 ) units' with no Math element and no UnsupportedMath diagnostic. '\\\\(n\\\\)2' displays '(n)2'. The source is preserved exactly; only recognition and rendering fail.
- **Existing tests**: Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:51 covers only '\\\\(\\\\sqrt[3]{x}+\\\\alpha\\\\)', with no inner spaces and no digit after the closer. No test covers '\\\\( x \\\\)' or '\\\\(n\\\\)2'.

### math-parser-04

**Trailing '\\\\' before \\end is rejected for multi-column environments but silently adds a phantom row for single-column ones**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:121` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: Matrix() parses one cell per loop iteration and, after a row separator, resets rowColumns and immediately parses the next cell. A trailing '\\\\' before '\\end{...}' (accepted and ignored by KaTeX/LaTeX, and very common in pasted LaTeX for cases/aligned/pmatrix) makes the loop parse an empty cell at '\\end{', so rowColumns is 1 for that phantom row. The final consistency check `columns != rowColumns` then rejects every environment with two or more columns, while a one-column environment passes the check and keeps a third, empty row that PreparedMath lays out with full row height (Place, Matrix branch, `y += std::max(size, above + below) + size * .3f`). The same formula is therefore either entirely literal or rendered with a blank row depending only on its column count.
- **Failure**: ParseMath(L"\\\\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\\\\\\\c&d\\\\\\\\\\\\end{pmatrix}"): after the second '\\\\\\\\', rows=2, rowColumns=0; Row(cell) returns an empty row at '\\\\end{', cells.size()=5, rowColumns=1; line 121 sees columns(2) != rowColumns(1) and rejects, so the whole matrix stays literal source with the UnsupportedMath diagnostic. ParseMath(L"\\\\begin{matrix}a\\\\\\\\b\\\\\\\\\\\\end{matrix}") is accepted as a 3x1 matrix whose last cell is an empty Row, rendering a visible empty row. '\\\\begin{cases} x & y \\\\\\\\ \\\\end{cases}' and '\\\\begin{aligned} a &= b \\\\\\\\ \\\\end{aligned}' are both rejected.
- **Fix**: After consuming '\\\\\\\\', skip whitespace and, if the next token is '\\\\end{', drop the pending empty row (do not push a cell) for every environment, matching KaTeX. Alternatively reject a trailing separator uniformly, but the permissive behaviour is what pasted LaTeX needs. Add tests for the two-column and one-column trailing-separator cases.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: '\$\$\\\\begin{cases} x & x>0 \\\\\\\\ -x & x\\\\le 0 \\\\\\\\ \\\\end{cases}\$\$' is rejected as literal source with an UnsupportedMath diagnostic, although KaTeX accepts it. '\$\$\\\\begin{matrix}a\\\\\\\\b\\\\\\\\\\\\end{matrix}\$\$' and the gathered equivalent are accepted and drawn with an extra empty row of about 1.3 em. Source is preserved in both cases.
- **Existing tests**: Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:76 rejects '\\\\begin{matrix}a&b\\\\\\\\c\\\\end{matrix}' (inconsistent columns, no trailing separator). Lines 19-43 cover aligned/cases/matrix geometry and gathered multi-column rejection, all without a trailing '\\\\\\\\'. No test covers a trailing row separator.

### math-parser-05

**A rejected inline formula overwrites its parent element's title, clobbering the UIA HelpText of an enclosing link**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:344` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.5

- **What**: On ParseMath failure or capacity rejection the builder writes the diagnostic text into `document->elements_[visit.parent].title`. visit.parent is whatever element currently owns the inline: a Paragraph, Heading, Cell, or a Link element (line 379 `parent = element;` and 385 `child.parent = parent;` route link children through the link element). SemanticElement::title is the UIA HelpText (DxUi.RichAccessibility.h:257 `element.title.empty() ? element.destination : element.title`), and for links it is first filled from the Markdown title at line 357. The math branch replaces that value, so a bad formula inside a link replaces the link's help text (and suppresses the destination fallback) with a math message; two rejected formulas in one paragraph silently share one title; [...]
- **Failure**: RichMarkdown source '[see \$\\\\unknown{x}\$]\(https://example.com "Reference")'. The Link element gets title 'Reference' at line 357, then the Math child is visited with visit.parent == link element; ParseMath rejects '\\\\unknown{x}', line 344 sets the link's title to 'Unsupported or oversized native equation. Exact source is preserved.' A screen reader reading HelpText for the link now hears the math diagnostic instead of 'Reference', and without a title it would have heard the diagnostic instead of the destination URL.
- **Fix**: Create a dedicated element for the rejected formula (e.g. SemanticRole::Math with a `rejected` flag, or the existing Code-flagged source span owning its own element) and put the diagnostic title on that element, as the diagram fallback does at line 537 for its own block element. At minimum, never overwrite a non-empty parent title and never target a Link/Image parent.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: RichMarkdown `[see $\unknown{x}$] (https://example.com "Reference")`: the Link element's title 'Reference' (set at RichSemanticDocument.cpp:357) is replaced at line 344 with 'Unsupported or oversized native equation. Exact source is preserved.' UIA HelpText for the hyperlink now gives the math diagnostic. Without a Markdown title, it gives the diagnostic instead of the destination URL (RichAccessibility.h:257).
- **Existing tests**: None pins it. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:78-80 checks only the retained display and that diagnostics are non-empty for `$\input{private}$`.

### math-parser-06

**MathNode/MathExpression declare copy operations and no move operations, so every std::move in the parser deep-copies subtrees**  
`include/DxUi/RichMath.h:35` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.12

- **What**: `MathNode(const MathNode&) = default; MathNode& operator=(const MathNode&) = default;` are user-declared copy operations, which suppress the implicit move constructor and move assignment ([class.copy.ctor]/8). Every `std::move(...)` of a MathNode in the parser therefore resolves to the copy constructor and clones the whole subtree (wstring + recursive vector\<MathNode>): Row's `super = std::move(argument)`, `sub = std::move(argument)`, `Make(MathKind::Scripts, L"", {std::move(atom), std::move(sub), std::move(super)})`, `children.push_back(std::move(atom))`, `Make(MathKind::Row, L"", std::move(children))` (the vector itself moves, but braced-init-list construction always copies its elements), Atom's `index = std::move(parsed.root)`. [...]
- **Failure**: ParseMath on a formula with 24 nested braces and ~500 nodes: each nesting level copies its entire subtree into the parent's initializer list, then ParseMath copies the root, then make\_shared copies it again; observed cost is roughly depth x nodes x 3 wstring/vector allocations rather than ~nodes. Under the allocation-failure sweeps the repository runs, each extra copy is another failure point that changes which formula fails.
- **Fix**: Add `MathNode(MathNode&&) noexcept = default; MathNode& operator=(MathNode&&) noexcept = default;` (and the same for MathExpression), change the MathExpression constructor to take `MathNode&&`, return `{std::move(root), L""}` from ParseMath, and replace the braced-init-list child vectors in Make with a small helper that reserves and push\_back(std::move(...))s. Keep the intentionally throwing default constructors that the test's static\_assert pins.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: A deeply nested formula near the 512-node / depth-24 limits deep-copies each subtree once per enclosing level (initializer\_list plus vector construction, Scripts copy-assignment, push\_back), then twice more for the whole tree (ParseMath return and make\_shared). The cost is O(depth x nodes), at most about 12k node copies times a small constant per formula per semantic rebuild, rather than O(nodes). Vector reallocation adds only amortized linear copies, not O(n^2).
- **Existing tests**: Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:48 static\_assert pins only that the default constructors are not nothrow. No test pins the absence of move operations, and no allocation-count test covers ParseMath.
