### math-parser-08

**\\limits and \\nolimits silently become an empty Space node that then serves as the base of the following scripts**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:240` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: `if (name == L"limits" || name == L"nolimits") return Make(MathKind::Space);` returns a zero-width Space atom. Row attaches any following `_`/`^` to that atom (lines 389-412), so the scripts are stacked on an invisible zero-width base after the operator rather than being positioned on the operator (above/below for \\limits, side for \\nolimits). The two commands are indistinguishable in output, the target shape for M13 ('limits(int,0,1)') is not produced, and there is no large-operator notion anywhere in MathKind, so 'accepted' here means 'ignored' rather than 'unsupported', which conflicts with the all-or-nothing policy's intent of never rendering something misleading.
- **Failure**: ParseMath(L"\\\\int\\\\limits\_0^1 x\\\\,dx") yields Row[Text "∫", Scripts(Space "", Text "0", Text "1"), Text "x", Space, Text "dx"]; PreparedMath places 0 and 1 at the same x immediately after the integral sign (Measure(Space) width 0), i.e. exactly the \\nolimits rendering. ParseMath(L"\\\\sum\\\\nolimits\_{i=1}^{n}") renders identically to \\\\sum\\\\limits\_{i=1}^{n}.
- **Fix**: Either reject \\limits/\\nolimits as unsupported until a MathKind::Limits (or a Scripts flag) exists, or implement them properly: when the previous child is a Text leaf, re-attach the scripts to that leaf and record a 'limits' flag that Measure/Place honour by centering the scripts above/below. Update docs/rich-text.md 'large operators/limits' wording accordingly.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: ParseMath(L"\\\\int\\\\limits\_0^1 x\\\\,dx") succeeds and renders 0/1 as side scripts immediately after ∫, identical to `\int_0^1` and `\int\nolimits_0^1`. `\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}` and `\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}` render the same. A bare `\limits_0^1` is also accepted as scripts on an invisible base instead of being rejected.
- **Existing tests**: None for \\limits/\\nolimits. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:72 checks only that `\int_0^1 x dx` parses (a bool check).

### math-parser-09

**\\sqrt[...] index is cut at the first ']' and parsed by a fresh recursive ParseMath with its own node/depth budget**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:200` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: The optional root index is extracted with `source.find(L']', ++at)` and handed to a brand-new `ParseMath(source.substr(at, end - at))`. Consequences: any ']' inside the index terminates it regardless of brace nesting, so a nested root or a `\text{]}` in the index rejects the whole formula; an empty index `\sqrt[]{x}` (legal in KaTeX) is rejected because ParseMath rejects empty source; the inner parser starts with `nodes = 0` and `depth = 0`, so the parse-time 512/24 caps are per-parser and only the post-parse count enforces them for the combined tree; and the inner result is re-validated with IsValidUtf16 and deep-copied via `index = std::move(parsed.root)` (copy, see math-parser-06). [...]
- **Failure**: ParseMath(L"\\\\sqrt[\\\\sqrt[3]{2}]{x}"): find finds the ']' after '3', the index source is '\\\\sqrt[3', the inner ParseMath finds no ']' and rejects, so the outer formula is rejected although KaTeX renders it. ParseMath(L"\\\\sqrt[]{x}") is rejected because the inner source is empty. A formula with 300 nodes in the index and 300 nodes in the radicand passes both Make() counters (each parser counts to 300) and is only rejected by the post-parse walk after full allocation.
- **Fix**: Parse the index in place: add an `index` terminator mode to Row (like `cell`/`right`) that stops at an unnested ']' at the current depth, called as `Row(depth + 1, false, false, false, /*index*/ true)`, allowing an empty index; this removes the recursive ParseMath, keeps one node/depth budget, and respects brace nesting.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: ParseMath(L"\\\\sqrt[{\\\\sqrt[3]{2}}]{x}") and ParseMath(L"\\\\sqrt[\\\\text{]}]{x}") are rejected because the index is cut at a brace-protected ']'. KaTeX and LaTeX accept both. ParseMath(L"\\\\sqrt[]{x}") is rejected because the inner source is empty, while KaTeX renders a plain root. An index and a radicand of about 300 nodes each pass both per-parser Make() counters. The combined tree is rejected only by the post-walk at line 449, after it has been fully built. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:68 checks only that `\sqrt[3]{x}` parses. No test covers a nested, brace-protected or empty index, or the combined index+radicand budget.

### math-parser-10

**Node-count and depth limits are enforced three times with different semantics (Make counter, ParseMath post-walk, semantic builder re-walk)**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:440` · simplification · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.11

- **What**: The 512-node limit is checked (1) in Make() via `++nodes > 512`, which over-counts merged Text atoms that are discarded at line 414 and under-counts the default-constructed `sub`/`super`/`index` nodes that become children without passing through Make; (2) by the recursive `count` lambda in ParseMath (lines 440-450) that re-walks the finished tree with its own `depth > 24` check, duplicating the guards already in Atom (177) and GroupText (87); and (3) by an identical recursive lambda in DxUi.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:321-328 that walks the tree a third time to compute `records` for the 4096-record document budget. [...]

In a document with 200 valid formulas, formulas 129-200 are parsed in full and then rejected by the object cap.
- **Fix**: Make the parse-time counter exact (count the two script slots and the root-index slot when the Scripts/Root node is made, do not count merged atoms), store `nodeCount` in MathExpression, delete both post-parse walks, and in the builder check `maths.size() + ... < MaximumObjects` and the record budget before calling ParseMath.

A formula wrapping `x^1` in 23 nested braces passes the Atom depth guard and is rejected by the post-walk's depth check. [...]
- **Existing tests**: No test covers the 512-node or 24-depth boundary or the object-cap ordering. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:81-84 covers only the 4,096-source-unit oversize fallback.

### math-parser-11

**Matrix row/column/cell caps are checked after the cell has been parsed and pushed**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:112` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: `cells.push_back(Row(depth + 1, false, true)); if (++rowColumns > 16 || cells.size() > 128) Reject();` parses and allocates the 17th column / 129th cell (including all of its nested nodes) before the admission check, and `++rows > 16` at line 135 is likewise evaluated after the whole row was parsed. The repository rule and the profile's capacityPolicy ('Check generated row/cell expansion before allocation') require the check first. Impact is limited because Make() caps total nodes at 512, but the order is still inverted and the check sits in the middle of the loop body rather than at the separator.
- **Failure**: ParseMath on a one-row matrix with 17 cells each containing a 20-node fraction tree: the 17th cell's 20 nodes are allocated and then the formula is rejected; with the 128-cell variant the same happens for the 129th cell.
- **Fix**: Move the checks to before parsing: on seeing '&' reject if `rowColumns == 16 || cells.size() == 128`, on seeing '\\\\\\\\' reject if `rows == 16`, then parse the next cell. Reserve `cells` to min(columns \* 16, 128) once the first row is known.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: A one-row matrix with 17 cells, each a roughly 20-node fraction, has its 17th cell fully parsed and pushed before line 112 rejects it. With 8 complete 16-column rows, the 129th cell is parsed before rejection. After a 1-column first row, a second row of up to 16 cells is parsed before the column-mismatch rejection. Rows are NOT over-allocated: line 135 rejects at the 16th `\\` separator, before the 17th row is parsed. Total waste stays within the 512-node Make() budget.
- **Existing tests**: Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:76 checks that `\begin{matrix}a&b\\c\end{matrix}` (column mismatch) is rejected, and :43 that a two-column gathered environment is rejected. No test covers the 16-column, 16-row or 128-cell caps or when they are checked.

### math-parser-12

**'\\\\\\\\' outside an environment is silently accepted as a literal backslash instead of being rejected**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:358` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: In Atom, a backslash followed by a non-letter falls into the `name.empty()` branch; the escaped unit '\\\\' is in the set `L"{}%_$&#\\"` and becomes a Text leaf containing a single backslash. Row only treats '\\\\\\\\' as a row separator when `cell` is true, so a TeX line break in plain display math or inside a group within a cell is rendered as a visible backslash glyph rather than being rejected as unsupported (the parser has no multi-line Row concept). This contradicts the 'Unsupported ... input is all-or-nothing' comment in RichMath.h:60 and silently changes the formula's meaning.
- **Failure**: ParseMath(L"a = b \\\\\\\\ c = d") succeeds with a Text leaf 'a=b\\\\c=d' and PreparedMath draws 'a=b\\\\c=d' on one line; KaTeX renders two lines in display mode. Inside a matrix, '\\\\begin{matrix}{a\\\\\\\\b}\\\\end{matrix}' renders the cell as 'a\\\\b' because the inner grouped Row has cell=false.
- **Fix**: Remove '\\\\' from the literal-escape set (TeX has \\backslash for the glyph) so '\\\\\\\\' outside a cell row hits Reject(), or implement multi-row display math (gathered-like) explicitly. Add '\\\\backslash' to the symbol table if the glyph is wanted.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Markdown `$$a = b \\ c = d$$` (RichMarkdown) gives ParseMath the raw content `a = b \\ c = d`. It succeeds with Row{Text "a=b\\c=d"} and is drawn on one line with a visible backslash and no UnsupportedMath diagnostic, whereas KaTeX breaks the line. `\begin{matrix}{a\\b}\end{matrix}` and `\left( a \\ b \right)` inside a cell likewise render `a\b`, because the grouped or delimited inner Row has cell=false.
- **Existing tests**: None. No test in Tests/ uses `\\\\` in math outside a matrix, cases, aligned or gathered environment, and none pins the literal-backslash behavior either way.

### math-parser-13

**Adjacent Text atoms are merged into one run, fusing function names and symbols with their operands**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:413` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: Row concatenates every consecutive MathKind::Text atom into the previous leaf (`children.back().text.append(atom.text)`). Named functions (\\sin, \\lim, \\log ...) and symbol commands are also emitted as Text, so there is no boundary between a function name and its argument, no thin space, and no way for PreparedMath to render functions upright and variables italic (Gather only italicises under \\mathit). The merge also hides structure from any future selection/hit-testing or accessibility work on the tree.
- **Failure**: ParseMath(L"\\\\sin x + \\\\log\\\\log n") produces the leaves 'sinx+loglogn' as one DirectWrite run, drawn as the word 'sinx+loglogn' in Cambria Math; KaTeX renders 'sin x + log log n' with thin spaces and upright operator names.
- **Fix**: Emit named functions as a distinct node (e.g. MathKind::Text with a 'function' flag or a new MathKind::Operator) that is never merged and gets a thin space after it in Measure/Place, and merge only identifier/digit/punctuation atoms. Keep the merge otherwise (it keeps leaf count low) but document that it is a rendering concession.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: ParseMath(L"\\\\sin x + \\\\log\\\\log n") returns Row{Text "sinx+loglogn"}. PreparedMath shapes it as one upright DirectWrite layout, so it reads as the word "sinx+loglogn" with no thin space after an operator name or between operator names. Removing the merge in Row() alone would not fix the picture, because PreparedMath::Place/Measure lay Row children edge to edge. The fix needs an operator or function marker on the node and spacing in Measure/Place. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:29 (TestRichMathAlignedCasesAndMatrixGeometry) relies on the merge: glyphs.size()==4 requires "=1" and "=1234" to be single leaves. No test covers named-function spacing.

### math-parser-14

**Space() uses locale-dependent iswspace, so parse results can vary with the process locale**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:22` · bug · minor · refuted (refute: refuted; reproduce: refuted) · plan: not scheduled (refuted)

- **What**: `while (at < source.size() && iswspace(source[at])) ++at;` classifies units through the CRT's current LC\_CTYPE. On MSVC, units below 256 use the locale table (U+00A0 NBSP is not a space in the "C" locale but is in Western locales), and units at or above 256 go through GetStringTypeW (U+3000 ideographic space is skipped, which TeX would not do). The inline scanner deliberately uses the ASCII-only SourceSpace() for its edge rules (MarkdownInlines.h:92-95), and the repository's Unicode policy expects deterministic handling; a consumer that calls setlocale(LC\_ALL, "") on its UI thread changes whether '\$a b\$' parses.
- **Failure**: The same document parsed in a process with the default "C" locale rejects nothing for '\$a + b\$' (NBSP becomes a Text leaf, merged into 'a + b'), while in a process that set a Western locale the NBSPs are skipped and the merged leaf is 'a+b'; glyph positions, widths and the accessible notation differ between the two.
- **Fix**: Replace iswspace with an explicit set (' ', '\\t', '\\n', '\\r', optionally '\\f'/'\\v') matching SourceSpace() in the scanner, and add a test with U+00A0 and U+3000 asserting locale-independent results.
- **Verifier (refute, refuted)**: The finding rests on iswspace depending on LC\_CTYPE in the MSVC CRT. The UCRT source in the installed Windows SDK shows it does not. - In the UCRT, iswspace(c) is iswctype(c, \_SPACE): corecrt\_wctype.h:133, and ucrt/convert/\_wctype.cpp:83-85 for the exported function. \_iswspace\_l likewise ignores its locale argument (corecrt\_wctype.h:147). - ucrt/convert/iswctype.cpp:28-55 states that the iswctype functions "are neither locale nor codepage dependent". [...]
- **Existing tests**: None. No test exercises NBSP or U+3000 inside math, or a non-C locale.

### math-parser-15

**Atom() is ~194 lines with an inline 106-entry linear symbol table, and Delimiter() duplicates part of that table**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:176` · simplification · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.11

- **What**: Atom (lines 176-369) handles grouping, every construct command, the entire symbol/function table as a local `constexpr std::pair<std::wstring_view, std::wstring_view> symbols[]` searched linearly on every backslash command, escapes, and literal units. Delimiter (141-175) re-implements the langle/rangle/lbrace/rbrace/vert/Vert mapping that already exists at 330-335. Because the table is a local in a function, nothing outside (tests, the target JSON pin, docs generation) can enumerate the supported command set, which is one reason math-parser-07's list drift went unnoticed.
- **Failure**: Before: adding \\tilde requires touching the accent `if` chain at 222-224 and PreparedMath's accent branch, adding \\lfloor requires editing both the symbol table and Delimiter, and no test can assert the supported list. After: a single sorted static table `{name, kind, glyph, delimiterRole}` in an internal header consumed by Atom, Delimiter, a `SupportedMathCommands()` test accessor and the corpus pin test.
- **Fix**: Split Atom into Group/Command/Escape/Literal helpers; hoist the table to a namespace-scope sorted `std::array` with `std::ranges::lower_bound`, mark entries that are legal delimiters so Delimiter() uses the same table; expose the table (Detail namespace) so a test can diff it against DxUiMath1-target.json.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: This is a maintainability issue only; behaviour is unaffected. The 106-entry symbol table is local to Atom and repeated by hand in Delimiter for six delimiter names, so the supported command list cannot be enumerated or pinned against DxUiMath1-target.json. Hoisting it into a namespace-scope table shared with Delimiter, and exposing it to tests, removes the duplication. New accents and delimiters would still need matching PreparedMath Fence/accent drawing code.
- **Existing tests**: No test enumerates or pins the command list. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:66-77 parses a few sample formulas only.

### math-parser-16

**PreparedMath re-measures subtrees repeatedly with a linear leaf lookup instead of caching extents**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:191` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.11

- **What**: Place() calls `Measure(node, size)` for the node it is placing and then Measure() again for each child before recursing into Place(child), which itself re-measures; Matrix Measure walks every cell twice (width and height passes, lines 92-104) and Matrix Place three more times (259, 266, 273); every Text measurement does `std::ranges::find(glyphs, &node, &Glyph::node)` (line 57), a linear search over all leaves. Complexity is O(nodes x depth x leaves) per final Advance, and Advance also creates a fresh IDWriteTextFormat for every leaf (line 318) although leaves share (size, bold, italic) triples. All of this is bounded by the 512-node/128-cell limits, so it is not unbounded work, but it is avoidable and runs once per formula per preparation and again on every reflow/device replacement.
- **Failure**: A 128-cell 16x8 matrix whose cells each hold a fraction with scripts (~500 nodes): the final Advance step performs on the order of 10^5 Measure calls and ~10^6 pointer comparisons in Leaf() during one cooperative turn, versus ~500 with cached extents.
- **Fix**: Assign each MathNode a pre-order index during Gather and keep `std::vector<Extent> extents` plus `std::vector<std::size_t> leafIndex` so Measure is computed once bottom-up and Place reads cached values; cache one IDWriteTextFormat per distinct (size, bold, italic) triple.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Within the enforced caps (512 nodes including empty script/index children, tree depth 24, 128 cells), the final Advance step of one formula re-measures subtrees O(depth) times. Each matrix level doubles the visits to its cells, and every leaf visit scans the glyph vector linearly. A realistic 128-cell matrix costs about 10^4 Measure visits and up to about 5×10^5 pointer comparisons in one cooperative turn, sub-millisecond. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:24-27 only checks the turn count (one Advance per leaf plus the final step, at most 16 turns). No test measures Measure or Place cost or counts text formats.

### math-parser-17

**An unclosed '\$\$' is handled inconsistently: silent literal text in short paragraphs, whole-paragraph source takeover with a diagnostic in long ones**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.MarkdownInlines.h:1237` · bug · minor · disputed (refute: refuted; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: For a display opener with no closer the scan loop runs to `min(text_.size(), content + 4097)`. If the paragraph has fewer than 4097 units after the opener, the branch falls through and '\$\$' is emitted as ordinary text with no diagnostic (docs/rich-text.md says nothing about unclosed display math; the target envelope says 'missing close owns body through EOF with diagnostic'). If the paragraph is longer, line 1237-1245 appends the entire remainder of the paragraph (`display ? text_.size() : ...`) as one Math node whose value exceeds 4096 units, ParseMath rejects it and every link, emphasis and code span in the rest of the paragraph is lost to a Code-flagged source span with an UnsupportedMath diagnostic. [...]
- **Failure**: Typing '\$\$' at the start of a 200-unit paragraph renders a literal '\$\$' and the rest normally; typing the same '\$\$' at the start of a 5000-unit paragraph (a long prose paragraph with links) replaces all of its formatting with monospaced source and raises a diagnostic, and the author must scroll to find the stray delimiter.
- **Fix**: Pick one policy and document it: either always leave an unclosed display opener literal (and cap the closer scan at 4097 units without taking over the remainder, relying on the scan bound alone for O(n) behaviour), or always emit a MarkdownDiagnostic for an unclosed display opener. Update the target JSON envelope and docs/rich-text.md to match.
- **Verifier (refute, refuted)**: The traced behaviour matches the finding, but both outcomes are what the documented and tested contract specifies. Short case: in Scan (DxUi.MarkdownInlines.h:1207-1248), an opener with no closer whose remaining leaf is 4096 units or less falls out of the math branch, and the '\$' is appended as one-unit Text (1358-1359). [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:81-84 pins the over-capacity takeover policy for inline single-dollar math. The short unclosed '\$\$' fallthrough has no dedicated test.

### math-parser-18

**No test pins ParseMath or the inline recognizer against the target corpus or the edge rules**  
`Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:66` · test-gap · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: T1

- **What**: Parser coverage is nine accepted strings and five rejected strings (lines 66-77) plus the three-environment alignment oracle (15-44) and one document with '\$5 and \$10' currency (49-56). Nothing loads DxUiMath1-target.json's 32 cases or its command lists, nothing checks the 4096/512/24/16x16/128 limits at their boundaries (e.g. 4096 vs 4097 units, 16 vs 17 columns, 128 vs 129 cells), and none of the behaviours in math-parser-01/02/03/04/08/12/17 ('\\\\(' with spaces, code span after a currency dollar, trailing '\\\\\\\\', \\\\limits placement, '\\\\\\\\' outside environments, newline inside \\\\text{}, unclosed '\$\$') has a test, which is why they could drift unnoticed. [...]
- **Failure**: Any of the fixes above, or a future table edit, can silently change accepted/rejected sets; today a change that broke \\\\widehat or \\\\mathbb would only be caught if it also broke one of the nine listed strings.
- **Fix**: Add a Scope.Tests.MathCorpus.h that reads DxUiMath1-target.json (the Mermaid corpus already has such pins), asserts prepared/malformed/unsupported per case id, asserts every listed command parses (after reconciling math-parser-07), and adds boundary tests for each numeric limit and for the inline recognizer edge rules listed above.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: As stated. One nuance: the over-capacity fallback for inline '\$' is pinned at 5000 units (lines 81-84), but there is no exact-boundary test (4096 vs 4097), no display-variant capacity test, and no test of any per-formula or per-document structural limit.
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:15-44 (alignment geometry), 45-84 (9 accepted / 5 rejected formulas, rejected-math fallback diagnostic, one 5000-unit over-capacity line); [...]

## Math layout fidelity (22)

### math-layout-01

**A newline inside \\text{} or an over-wide leaf drops the whole document to source view instead of a per-formula fallback**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:332` · bug · major · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.2

- **What**: PreparedMath::Advance shapes each Text leaf with a wrapping IDWriteTextFormat (CreateTextFormat default WRAP, max width 65536) and then requires exactly one line: `const auto hr = leaf.layout->GetLineMetrics(&line, 1, &count); if (FAILED(hr) || count != 1) throw std::length_error("Mathematical text leaf must be one line");` (lines 332-334), and later `if (extent.width > 65536 ...) throw std::length_error("Mathematical geometry capacity exceeded")` (339-340). The parser never rejects control characters inside `\text{...}`/`\operatorname{...}` (GroupText, NativeMath.cpp 69-101 copies any unit including \\n and \\r), and display math is documented to span logical lines (docs/rich-text.md 1221). [...]
- **Failure**: Document `Intro paragraph.\n\n$$\n\begin{cases} 1 & \text{if x is\npositive} \\ 0 & \text{otherwise}\end{cases}\n$$\n\nMore text.` in RichMarkdown rich mode: the first Text leaf contains '\\n', DWrite lays out two lines, Advance throws std::length\_error, the editor clears the semantic document and every paragraph (including the unrelated ones) is rendered as raw Markdown source. Same outcome for `$\text{` + 4000 'W' + `}$` (≈70,800 DIP at 18.4 px exceeds the 65,536 wrap width, so the leaf wraps). [...]
- **Fix**: Enforce the limits at parse time and per formula: reject (or normalise to a space) \\r/\\n/control units inside \\text/\\operatorname in GroupText; set DWRITE\_WORD\_WRAPPING\_NO\_WRAP on the leaf format and cap leaf width against a per-formula budget before CreateTextLayout; in NativeImageGeometry::PrepareMath catch the math capacity failure and demote only that element (keep the ￼ object out, emit the literal source span with SemanticDiagnosticKind::UnsupportedMath) or at minimum throw NativeObjectCapacity so the editor keys the rejection to the layout (font/width) rather than to the syntax.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: The newline scenario holds, with one correction: the '\\n' is in the second leaf, "if x is\\npositive" (after "1"), not the first. Any display formula with a line break inside \\text{...}/\\operatorname{...}, e.g. "\$\$\\text{first\\nsecond}\$\$", turns the whole document into source geometry and reports SourceFallback, with no math diagnostic. A U+2028 inside \\text in single-line inline \$...\$ gives the same result, because GroupText copies it and DirectWrite breaks the line there. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None covers this. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp TestRichSemanticNativeMathematicsAndBroaderMermaid tests only parse-level rejection (\$\\input{private}\$) and the oversized-source literal fallback; TestRichMathAlignedCasesAndMatrixGeometry uses single-line leaves.

### math-layout-02

**Variables are never set in math italic; every identifier renders upright**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:41` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C4

- **What**: Gather(node, size, bold, italic = false) only turns italic on for a Style node named `mathit` (`node.kind == MathKind::Style ? node.text == L"mathit" : italic`, lines 51-52) and the root is gathered with `Gather(expression->root, font, false)` (line 310). TeX/KaTeX set Latin letters and lowercase Greek in math italic by default and keep digits, operators and function names upright. Here Text atoms (which the parser also merges with neighbouring digits/operators, NativeMath.cpp 413-414) are shaped upright in Cambria Math, so a formula is visually indistinguishable from plain text.
- **Failure**: `$E=mc^2$` (the gallery tile, DxUi.Tests.Gallery.cpp 1101) renders as upright 'E=mc' with an upright '2' superscript; `$f(x)=\alpha x+\beta$` renders f, x, α, β upright. KaTeX/Word render E, m, c, f, x, α, β in italic.
- **Fix**: Classify Text atoms in the parser (identifier vs number vs operator vs function/text) and, for identifier leaves outside \\text/\\mathrm/\\operatorname, map ASCII letters and lowercase Greek to the Mathematical Italic alphanumerics (U+1D434.., using the same Alphabet() mechanism already used for \\mathbb/\\mathcal, with the reserved code points such as U+210E for italic h) so Cambria Math supplies true italic glyphs; keep digits, operators and named functions upright.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: \$E=mc^2\$ is shaped as leaves "E=m", "c" and superscript "2", all upright. \$f(x)=\\alpha x+\\beta\$ renders f, x, α and β upright. \$\\mathrm{d}x\$ and \$dx\$ look the same, so the advertised roman style has no visible effect. Only \\mathit gives a slant, and Cambria Math has no italic face, so DirectWrite synthesises an oblique.
- **Existing tests**: No test pins identifier style. The gallery tile at Tests/Controls/DxUi.Tests.Gallery.cpp:1101 shows E=mc^2 upright in its baseline capture.

### math-layout-03

**No atom-class spacing: adjacent atoms are merged into one DWrite run, so bin/rel/op spacing and function-name spacing are lost**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:413` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C4

- **What**: Row() appends consecutive Text atoms into one leaf: `if (atom.kind == MathKind::Text && ! children.empty() && children.back().kind == MathKind::Text) children.back().text.append(atom.text);` (413-414). Symbol commands (\\alpha, \\leq, \\sum, \\sin ...) all become MathKind::Text (350-352), so the layout engine never sees separate atoms and cannot apply the TeXbook Appendix G inter-atom table (thin 3/18 em for op, medium 4/18 em around bin, thick 5/18 em around rel, none for ord/punct). Measure/Place for Row simply advance by each child's width (PreparedMath.h 126-134, 295-299).
- **Failure**: `$a+b=c$` becomes a single leaf "a+b=c" shaped as ordinary text: no medium space around '+', no thick space around '='. `$\sin x + \log n$` becomes "sinx+logn" with the function name glued to its argument. `$x\in A$` becomes "x∈A" with no rel spacing. KaTeX renders 'a + b = c', 'sin x', 'x ∈ A'.
- **Fix**: Stop merging in Row(); give MathNode an atom class (Ord, Op, Bin, Rel, Open, Close, Punct, Inner) assigned by the parser (symbol table entry, ASCII operator, function name => Op, Fraction/Delimited => Inner) and apply the 8x8 TeX spacing table (suppressed in script styles) in Row Measure/Place. Shaping one run per atom also gives correct italic correction and lets \\sin x get its thin space.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: \$a+b=c\$ is one leaf "a+b=c", with only Cambria Math sidebearings around + and =. \$\\sin x + \\log n\$ is one leaf "sinx+logn", with the function names touching their arguments. \$x\\in A\$ is "x∈A" with no relation spacing.
- **Existing tests**: Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:21-29 TestRichMathAlignedCasesAndMatrixGeometry requires glyphs.size()==4 for `x&=1\\xxxx&=1234`. That relies on "=1" and "=1234" being merged single leaves, so a fix would have to update it. The test pins alignment, not the absence of spacing.

### math-layout-04

**Large operators and \\lim never take limits above/below; \\limits and \\nolimits are silently discarded**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:221` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C4

- **What**: Scripts placement is unconditional side-script placement: `Place(node.children[1], x + base.width, baseline + size * .35f, ...)` / `Place(node.children[2], x + base.width, baseline - size * .55f, ...)` (225-226). The parser turns `\limits`/`\nolimits` into an empty Space node (NativeMath.cpp 240-241) and \\sum/\\prod/\\int/\\lim into ordinary Text (280-285, 345), so there is no operator class, no display-style stacked limits, and no display-size operator glyph. Target case M13 in DxUiMath1-target.json (`\int\limits_0^1`, shape `limits(int,0,1)`) explicitly expects limits placement.
- **Failure**: `$$\sum_{i=1}^{n} i^2$$` renders as a text-size ∑ with 'i=1' and 'n' stacked to its right (like \\textstyle \\sum\\nolimits), never above/below; `$\lim_{x\to 0} f(x)$` renders 'lim' with 'x→0' as a right subscript although TeX puts the limit below 'lim' in every style; `\int\limits_0^1` renders exactly like `\int_0^1`.
- **Fix**: Add an Op atom class with a `limits` flag (default: display style for \\sum-type, always for \\lim/\\max/\\min/\\det/\\gcd, never for \\int) honoured by \\limits/\\nolimits; in Place, stack sub/super centred over/under the operator with MATH UpperLimitGapMin/LowerLimitGapMin; in display style use the operator's larger size variant (MATH DisplayOperatorMinHeight or a 1.4x scaled glyph).
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: \$\$\\sum\_{i=1}^{n} i^2\$\$ renders a text-size ∑ with i=1 and n as side scripts, not stacked. \$\$\\lim\_{x\\to 0} f(x)\$\$ in display math puts x→0 at the side instead of below. Inline \$\\lim\_{x\\to0}\$ with a side subscript is correct TeX text-style behaviour. \\int\\limits\_0^1 renders the same as \\int\_0^1, because \\limits is silently dropped.
- **Existing tests**: None. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:72 only checks that `\int_0^1 x dx` parses; no geometry test covers operator scripts.

### math-layout-05

**Superscript/subscript shifts are fixed fractions of the font size and ignore the base's height/depth**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:225` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C4

- **What**: Scripts are placed at `baseline + size * .35f` (sub) and `baseline - size * .55f` (super) regardless of the base box (`const auto base = Measure(node.children[0], size);` is used only for `x + base.width`). TeX raises the superscript to max(base.height - sup\_drop, sup1/sup2/sup3) and lowers the subscript to max(base.depth + sub\_drop, sub1/sub2), with a minimum gap between the two. Measure (81-85) merely enlarges the box so nothing clips, which hides the problem from the capacity checks.
- **Failure**: `$\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^2$`: the Delimited base is ≈1.5 em tall above the baseline, but the '2' is drawn with its baseline only 0.55 em up, i.e. beside the fraction bar, not at the top-right corner; `$\frac{a}{b}_x$` draws the subscript beside the denominator; `$\sum_{i}^{n}$` and `$x_i^n$` get identical shifts even though ∑ is much taller than x.
- **Fix**: Compute shifts from the measured base: superShift = max(base.above - SuperscriptBaselineDropMax, SuperscriptShiftUp[style]), subShift = max(base.below + SubscriptBaselineDropMin, SubscriptShiftDown), enforce SubSuperscriptGapMin, using Cambria Math MATH constants (or the TeXbook sigma values) scaled by size.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: RichMarkdown `$\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^2$` at size s: the base box reaches about 1.55s above the baseline, but the '2' is drawn with its baseline at 0.55s, so it sits beside the numerator or rule instead of the top-right corner. `$\frac{a}{b}_x$` places x at baseline + 0.35s, beside the denominator. Simple bases (x^2, a\_i) look acceptable. The ∑ example is not a separate failure, because ∑ is a text-size leaf with the same measured line box as x.
- **Existing tests**: None pins script placement. TestRichMathAlignedCasesAndMatrixGeometry (Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:15-44) checks only matrix, aligned and cases column x positions. TestRichSemanticNativeMathematicsAndBroaderMermaid only checks that `x_1^2` parses.

### math-layout-06

**Vertical metrics come from DWrite line boxes, not glyph ink or MATH constants; fractions are ~3 em tall and dfrac/tfrac/display are identical**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:335` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C4

- **What**: Each leaf's extent is `{metrics.widthIncludingTrailingWhitespace, line.baseline, metrics.height - line.baseline}` (line 335), i.e. the font's typo ascent/descent/lineGap (Cambria Math: 1593/455/353 of 2048 with USE\_TYPO\_METRICS set, verified in C:\\Windows\\Fonts\\cambria.ttc), so a lowercase 'x' leaf is ≈0.78-0.95 em above and ≈0.22-0.39 em below the baseline although its ink is 0.47 em high. Fraction Measure adds `size * .45f`/`size * .15f` and Place adds `size * .2f` gaps on each side of the rule (71-72, 201-205); fraction children are always 0.86x (Gather 48-50, Measure 71) with no display/text style, and the parser maps frac/dfrac/tfrac to one node kind (NativeMath.cpp 192-193). [...]
- **Failure**: Inline `$\frac{1}{2}$` in a 16 px Segoe UI paragraph: size = 18.4, numerator/denominator boxes ≈1.0 x size each, total object height ≈2.6 x size ≈ 48 DIP, so that one line grows from ≈21 DIP to ≈48 DIP (KaTeX inline \\frac is ≈1.6 em ≈ 26 px). `$\dfrac{1}{2}$`, `$\tfrac{1}{2}$` and `$$\frac{1}{2}$$` all render identically. Superscripts, roots and matrices inherit the same padding (`x^2` object is ≈1.44 x size tall).
- **Fix**: Measure leaves by ink: use IDWriteTextLayout::GetOverhangMetrics (or IDWriteFontFace::GetDesignGlyphMetrics via a custom IDWriteTextRenderer) to get the real height/depth, and read the MATH constants (AxisHeight, FractionNumeratorShiftUp/DisplayStyle, FractionDenominatorShiftDown, FractionNum/DenomGapMin, FractionRuleThickness) from Cambria Math via IDWriteFontFace::TryGetFontTable('MATH'). Add a MathStyle (Display/Text/Script/ScriptScript) threaded through Gather/Measure/Place so dfrac/tfrac and `$$` select the right constants and sizes.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Inline `$\frac{1}{2}$` in a 16 DIP RichMarkdown paragraph: PreparedMath size 18.4 with children at 15.8. Each leaf uses the Cambria Math line box (about 1.17 em), so the object is about 48 DIP tall and its line grows from about 21 to about 48 DIP. `$\dfrac{1}{2}$`, `$\tfrac{1}{2}$` and `$$\frac{1}{2}$$` produce identical geometry because there is no style input.
- **Existing tests**: None pins vertical metrics or fraction gaps. The only PreparedMath geometry test (Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:15-44) checks matrix column x alignment. TestRichSemanticNativeMathematicsAndBroaderMermaid only checks that `\frac12` parses.

### math-layout-07

**Stretchy delimiters are drawn as straight-line polylines; \\big-family is ignored, \\left. reserves full width and \| fences are asymmetric**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:140` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C4

- **What**: Fence() synthesises every delimiter from 2-7 line segments with the single stroke width used for rules: parentheses are three straight strokes with 20 % slanted ends (148-153), braces six strokes with a fixed ±2 DIP middle tick (154-162), angle brackets two strokes, bars/brackets vertical lines. No glyph variant or MATH vertical assembly is used although Cambria Math provides them. `\big(`, `\Big(`, `\bigg(`, `\Bigg(` are parsed into an ordinary Text glyph with no size change (NativeMath.cpp 235-239). A null delimiter `\left.` still adds the full `size * .8f` to the Delimited width (Measure 118-125) and the content starts at `x + size * .4f` (194). [...]
- **Failure**: `$\left( \frac{a}{b} \right)$` renders angular, hexagon-like parentheses instead of curved glyphs; `$\bigl(\ldots\bigr)$` is rejected entirely (unknown command) while `$\big( x \big)$` renders at normal size; `$\left. \frac{df}{dx} \right|_{x=0}$` shows a 0.4 em blank gap before the fraction; `$\left| x \right|$` has a 0.4 em gap on the left and 0.15 em on the right.
- **Fix**: Select delimiter glyphs from Cambria Math: read MATH MathVariants vertical GlyphConstruction for ( ) [ ] { } ⟨ ⟩ \| ‖ ⌊ ⌋ ⌈ ⌉ and either pick the smallest variant whose height covers the content (TeX delimiterfactor 901 / delimitershortfall 5pt) or assemble top/extender/bottom pieces; draw them as glyph runs (DrawGlyphRun under the same cached-run contract). Implement \\big/\\Big/\\bigg/\\Bigg (and the l/r/m variants) as explicit 1.2/1.8/2.4/3.0 em sizes; make '.' a null delimiter with \\nulldelimiterspace (1.2 pt) and compute symmetric slots for all shapes.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: - `$\left| x \right|$`: the left bar sits 0.4 em before x and the right bar 0.15 em after it, because '\|' is not in the `right` set so the closing bar takes the inner edge of its slot. - `$\left. \frac{df}{dx} \right|_{x=0}$`: an empty 0.4 em gap precedes the fraction. - `$\big( x \big)$`: parses and renders as one text-size leaf "(x)" with no enlargement. - Not defects: `\bigl(` keeps the whole formula as literal source with a diagnostic, which is documented. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None pins fence geometry. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:73 only checks that `\left( a \rightarrow b \right)` parses. No test covers \\big, \\left. or \| fence placement.

### math-layout-08

**Display math (\$\$…\$\$, \\[…\\]) is laid out exactly like inline math: no display style and no centering**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.MarkdownInlines.h:1234` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C4

- **What**: Scan() computes `display` (line 1212) only to decide the closer and the newline rule, then calls `Append(Kind::Math, first, cursor_, ...)` (1234) without recording it. SemanticBuilder::Inline emits the same `￼` object for every math node (DxUi.RichSemanticDocument.cpp 317-340) and PreparedMath has no style input (ctor at DxUi.PreparedMath.h 303-311). Paragraph alignment in geometry is only changed for table cells (DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp 729-734), so a display-math paragraph is leading-aligned at the same size as inline math.
- **Failure**: `$$x=\frac{-b\pm\sqrt{b^2-4ac}}{2a}$$` (the gallery sample) renders left-aligned at the inline size with text-style fraction/operators, whereas KaTeX/Markdown viewers centre it on its own line in display style with larger operators and limits above/below. `\[\sum_{k=0}^{n} k\]` is indistinguishable from `$\sum_{k=0}^{n} k$`.
- **Fix**: Carry a `display` flag on the Math inline node and SemanticElement (or a distinct SemanticRole::DisplayMath), pass MathStyle::Display into PreparedMath, and when a paragraph consists solely of a display object set DWRITE\_TEXT\_ALIGNMENT\_CENTER (respecting RTL) and add \\abovedisplayskip/\\belowdisplayskip spacing. Document the behaviour in docs/rich-text.md either way.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: RichMarkdown `$$x=\frac{-b+\sqrt{b^2-4ac}}{2a}$$` (the gallery sample) or `\[\sum_{k=0}^{n} k\]` produces the same Math object, size and geometry as the inline `$...$` or `\(...\)` form, leading-aligned in its paragraph. The published gallery capture shows this. docs/rich-text.md does not say that display math renders in inline style.
- **Existing tests**: TestRichSemanticNativeMathematicsAndBroaderMermaid (Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:45-63) asserts that inline, display and slash math each become one atomic Math object. It does not distinguish display from inline presentation, and no test pins centering or display style.

### math-layout-09

**Math is always prepared at body font x 1.15, so formulas in headings are far smaller than the heading text**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:147` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.12

- **What**: NativeImageGeometry creates every formula with `std::make_unique<PreparedMath>(write, maths[index].expression, font * 1.15f)` where `font` is `options.fontSizeDip` (State ctor, line 292). The surrounding DWrite run, however, is scaled per heading level in PrepareOwned: `layout->SetFontSize(fontSize * (1.0f + 0.15f * (7 - run.heading)), range)` (DxUi.RichTextLayout.cpp 270), i.e. H1 = 1.9x, H2 = 1.75x, H3 = 1.6x. The inline object metrics are fixed, so the formula does not scale with its run. The 1.15 constant also makes inline math 15 % larger than adjacent body text in ordinary paragraphs.
- **Failure**: `## Pythagoras: $a^2+b^2=c^2$` renders the heading glyphs at 1.75 x 16 = 28 px while the formula is set at 18.4 px, visibly shrunken on the heading line; conversely `Let $x$ be` renders the math x 15 % larger than the surrounding 'Let'.
- **Fix**: Resolve the effective run size for the object's display position (heading factor from the paragraph's runs) before constructing PreparedMath, and replace the 1.15 fudge by matching x-height or 1.0; re-prepare on heading changes just as on font changes (typography revision already keys the geometry).
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: `## Pythagoras: $a^2+b^2=c^2$` at 16 DIP body: heading glyphs are 1.75 x 16 = 28 DIP (RichTextLayout.cpp:270), while the formula leaves are shaped at 16 x 1.15 = 18.4 DIP (RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:147) and reserved by a fixed-metric placeholder (:775-776), so the formula is visibly smaller than its heading (~0.66x at H2, ~0.61x at H1). The body-text '15% larger' sub-claim is probably an intentional x-height compensation and is not by itself a defect.
- **Existing tests**: None pins it. Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:15-44 builds PreparedMath directly at 24.f and checks only relative glyph alignment; no test covers math inside a heading.

### math-layout-10

**\\epsilon/\\varepsilon and \\phi/\\varphi are swapped; \\cdot maps to MIDDLE DOT**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:248` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: The symbol table maps `epsilon` => U+03B5 and `varepsilon` => U+03F5 (lines 248-249) and `phi` => U+03C6, `varphi` => U+03D5 (265-266) (code points verified from the file). TeX, KaTeX (symbols.js) and MathML map \\epsilon to U+03F5 (lunate ϵ), \\varepsilon to U+03B5 (ε), \\phi to U+03D5 (ϕ) and \\varphi to U+03C6 (φ). `cdot` maps to U+00B7 MIDDLE DOT (line 288) instead of U+22C5 DOT OPERATOR, which has different spacing/vertical placement in Cambria Math.
- **Failure**: `$\epsilon$` renders the curly ε and `$\varepsilon$` the lunate ϵ, the opposite of every TeX renderer; a paper that uses \\phi for the golden ratio shows the open φ form; `$a\cdot b$` uses the text middle dot.
- **Fix**: Swap the two pairs and use U+22C5 for \\cdot; add a pinned symbol-table test comparing against the KaTeX 0.16.11 symbol list already stored under ThirdParty/Markdown.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: `$\epsilon > 0$` draws U+03B5 (the \\varepsilon form) and `$\varepsilon$` draws U+03F5. `$\phi$` draws U+03C6 (the \\varphi form) and `$\varphi$` draws U+03D5. `$a\cdot b$` draws U+00B7 MIDDLE DOT instead of U+22C5 DOT OPERATOR.
- **Existing tests**: None. Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:66-77 checks only that formulas parse or are rejected, never their code points.

### math-layout-11

**ASCII '-', '\*', "'", '%' and '~' are rendered literally instead of as minus, asterisk operator, prime, comment and nbsp**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:363` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: Atom() turns any non-special unit into a Text atom unchanged (`std::wstring value(1, c); ... return Make(MathKind::Text, std::move(value));`, 365-368). TeX math mode renders '-' as U+2212 MINUS SIGN, '\*' as U+2217 ASTERISK OPERATOR, a prime "'" as a superscript U+2032, treats '%' as a comment and '~' as a non-breaking space.
- **Failure**: `$a-b$` draws a short hyphen (U+002D) instead of a minus sign; `$f'(x)$` draws a straight apostrophe at the baseline instead of a raised prime; `$a % comment$` renders 'a%comment'; `$a~b$` renders a tilde glyph.
- **Fix**: Map '-' => U+2212, '\*' => U+2217, "'" (and runs of primes) => superscript Scripts with U+2032/U+2033, '~' => Space node, and treat '%' to end of line as a comment (or reject it explicitly so the source stays literal).
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: `$a-b$` shapes 'a-b' with U+002D (a short hyphen), not U+2212. `$f'(x)$` draws a baseline U+0027 apostrophe instead of a raised prime. `$a~b$` draws a tilde glyph. In display math, `$$a % note` + newline + `b$$` renders 'a%noteb' where TeX/KaTeX render 'ab'.
- **Existing tests**: None pins it. DxUiMath1-target.json case shapes such as M02 `row(b,-,c)` are notation-only, carry no code points, and the native tests do not execute them.

### math-layout-12

**Script and fraction sizes are fixed ratios that compound without a scriptscript floor**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:48` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C4

- **What**: Gather uses `size * (reduced ? node.kind == MathKind::Fraction ? 0.86f : 0.64f : 1.f)` for every nesting level (48-50) and Measure mirrors `.86f`/`.64f` (71, 76, 81). TeX uses 1.0 / 0.7 / 0.5 and stops at scriptscript (0.5); fraction parts drop one style (display => text 1.0, text => script 0.7) and never shrink further. There is no MathStyle, so nested scripts shrink geometrically.
- **Failure**: `$x^{a^{b^{c}}}$` sets c at 0.64^3 = 0.26 x size (≈4.8 px at 18.4 px) where TeX sets it at 0.5; `$\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{x}}}$` sets the innermost x at 0.64 x while TeX text style would use 0.7 for the first level and 0.5 thereafter; a single `$\frac{a}{b}$` in display style should be full size, here 0.86.
- **Fix**: Introduce MathStyle {Display, Text, Script, ScriptScript} with sizes 1/1/0.7/0.5, transitions sup/sub => next-smaller style (floored at ScriptScript), fraction num/den => Display->Text->Script->ScriptScript, root index => ScriptScript, smallmatrix => Script; use MATH ScriptPercentScaleDown/ScriptScriptPercentScaleDown when available.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: `$x^{a^{b^{c}}}$` at body 16 DIP (math 18.4) shapes c at 18.4 x 0.64^3 = 4.8 DIP, against TeX's scriptscript floor of 0.5 x (9.2 DIP). `$\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{x}}}$` sets x at 0.636x, against 0.5x in TeX text style. `\dfrac{a}{b}` gets 0.86x operands where display style keeps 1.0x.
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:15-44 (TestRichMathAlignedCasesAndMatrixGeometry) checks only alignment of same-size matrix cells. No test checks script or fraction scale factors or nesting floors.

### math-layout-13

**Spacing commands have wrong widths: \\, \\: \\; are equal, \\! is zero, \\quad/\\qquad are half size**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:356` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: `\,`, `\;`, `\:`, `\ ` all become a Space node with one unit and `\!` an empty Space (`Make(MathKind::Space, escaped == L'!' ? L"" : L" ")`, 356-357); `\quad` is two units and `\qquad` four (242-243). Measure gives `node.text.size() * size * 0.25f` (DxUi.PreparedMath.h 67-68). TeX: \\, = 3/18 em, \\: = 4/18 em, \\; = 5/18 em, \\! = -3/18 em, \\ = interword (~0.33 em), \\quad = 1 em, \\qquad = 2 em.
- **Failure**: `$\int_0^1 f(x)\,dx$` puts a 0.25 em gap before dx (TeX 0.167 em); `$a\!b$` shows no tightening; `$x \quad y$` separates by 0.5 em instead of 1 em and `\qquad` by 1 em instead of 2.
- **Fix**: Store the width in em (float or 18ths) on the Space node and let Measure return it directly, including negative widths for \\! (clamped so the row width stays >= 0).
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: As stated. `$\int_0^1 f(x)\,dx$` leaves 0.25 em before dx instead of 0.167 em. \\, \\: and \\; all look the same. `$a\!b$` gets no negative kern. `$x\quad y$` is 0.5 em apart instead of 1 em, and \\qquad gives 1 em instead of 2 em. Whitespace in math source is skipped (Parser::Space via iswspace), so these commands are the only way to set horizontal space.
- **Existing tests**: None pins spacing widths. Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:66-75 only checks that formulas parse ('\\int\_0^1 x dx', without \\,). TestRichMathAlignedCasesAndMatrixGeometry does not use Space nodes.

### math-layout-14

**Accents are full-width strokes with fixed DIP constants and no skew/centering; \\hat and \\widehat are identical**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:229` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C4

- **What**: Accent placement draws across the whole box (`Stroke(x, y + size * .15f, x + box.width / 2, y)` for hat, `Stroke(x, y, x + box.width, y)` for bar/overline) and uses absolute DIP sizes for \\vec arrowheads (4 x 3 DIP), \\dot (a 2 DIP tick) and \\ddot (+4..+6 DIP), lines 232-251. The accent height is `baseline - box.above + size * .1f` where box.above already includes the leaf's full font ascent, so the accent floats well above short letters. TeX centres a glyph accent over the base's skew centre (italic correction), uses AccentBaseHeight so accents over x-height letters sit low, and distinguishes \\hat (fixed glyph) from \\widehat (stretchy).
- **Failure**: `$\dot{x}$` at 18 px shows a 2 DIP bar high above x; at 36 px it is the same 2 DIP tick; `$\vec{v}$` draws a 4 DIP arrowhead regardless of size; `$\hat{x}$` and `$\widehat{x}$` are identical wide chevrons spanning the full advance including side bearings; `$\bar{f}$` is not shifted right over the italic f.
- **Fix**: Render accents as Cambria Math glyphs (U+0302 ^, U+02D9 ˙, U+00A8 ¨, U+20D7 ⃗, U+00AF ¯) scaled with the style size; position the accent at max(base ink height, MATH AccentBaseHeight) and horizontally at the base's top-accent attachment/skew centre; use the MATH vertical variants or a stretched glyph for \\widehat/\\widetilde over multi-letter bases.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Drop the `$\bar{f}$` italic-skew sub-scenario. Ordinary letters are not italic here: Gather passes italic=false and sets italic only under \\mathit, so the f is upright Cambria Math and needs no skew. The rest stands. `$\dot{x}$` draws a 2-DIP dash about 0.98 em above the baseline, about 0.5 em above x's ink. At 36 px it is still 2 DIP long, now about 1.8 DIP thick. The two \\ddot dots sit at w/2-1..w/2+1 and w/2+4..w/2+6, so the pair is 2.5 DIP right of centre. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None checks accent geometry. Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:69 only checks that '\\overline{AB}+\\vec{v}' parses.

### math-layout-15

**One fixed column/row gap for every environment: aligned gets a gap before '=', cases/matrix gaps differ from TeX, smallmatrix is full size**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:94` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C4

- **What**: Matrix Measure adds `widest + size * .65f` per column (94) and `+ size * .3f` per row (105); Place uses the same `widths[col] + size * .65f` (276) for all environments. In TeX `aligned` pairs its r/l columns with zero separation (the '&' is a zero-width alignment point) and uses 2 em between pairs; `cases` uses \\quad (1 em) between the value and the condition; matrices use 2 x \\arraycolsep (10 pt ≈ 1 em); `smallmatrix` is set in script style with tighter gaps. Place also floors each row's ascent at `size * .8f` (270) while Measure does not (98-105), so the measured box and the placed rows disagree whenever that floor matters.
- **Failure**: `\begin{aligned} x &= 1 \\ xxxx &= 1234 \end{aligned}` renders 'x = 1' with a 0.65 em hole between x and = (TeX: 'x = 1' with ordinary rel spacing); `\begin{smallmatrix} a & b \\ c & d \end{smallmatrix}` is as large as `matrix`; `\begin{cases}` rows are 0.3 em apart instead of \\jot + baselineskip.
- **Fix**: Give each environment a column-separation rule (aligned: 0 inside an rl pair, 2 em between pairs; cases: 1 em; matrix family: 1 em; smallmatrix: 0.33 em and Script style) and a row gap (\\jot 3 pt plus the row's ascent/descent); compute the row floor in one place used by both Measure and Place.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: `\begin{aligned} x &= 1 \\ xxxx &= 1234 \end{aligned}` renders x, a 0.65-em gap, then '=1' with no space after '='. TeX gives x, 0.278 em, '=', 0.278 em, 1. Matrix and cases columns are 0.65 em apart instead of about 1 em. smallmatrix is the same size as matrix. The row-gap sub-claim is overstated. A row advances by max(1 em, A+B) + 0.3 em, which is about 1.47 em for Cambria Math text leaves (typo line height 2401/2048 = 1.17 em), close to TeX's cases/aligned pitch of about 1.44-1.5 em. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:15-44 (TestRichMathAlignedCasesAndMatrixGeometry) pins right/left/centre column edges for aligned, cases and matrix only. It asserts no inter-column gap, no smallmatrix size and no Measure/Place row agreement.

### math-layout-16

**All rules share one stroke width and are not pixel-aligned; radical/fraction insets are DIP constants**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:364` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C4

- **What**: Draw strokes every line (fraction rule, radical, overline/underline, accents, delimiters) with `std::max(1.f, font / 20)` (365) under the object's scale transform, at unsnapped float coordinates (rule y = `baseline - size * .25f`, radical top = `baseline - box.above + 2`, fraction rule from `x + 2` to `x + box.width - 2`, lines 202-218). Cambria Math publishes separate FractionRuleThickness, RadicalRuleThickness, OverbarRuleThickness and UnderbarRuleThickness, and TeX's \\overline/\\sqrt rules are the default rule thickness (0.04 em), so 0.05 em is heavy for rules and too thin for delimiter strokes. With per-primitive antialiasing a 1 DIP line at a fractional y covers two device rows as a grey smear at 100 % DPI.
- **Failure**: `$\frac{a}{b}$` at 16 px body: the fraction bar is drawn 0.92 DIP wide (clamped to 1 DIP) at y ≈ k + 0.37, rendered as two half-grey pixel rows; `\left(` strokes are the same 1 DIP as the fraction bar, so large parentheses look spidery; the `+2`/`-2` DIP insets do not scale with font size.
- **Fix**: Read the rule thicknesses from the MATH table (fall back to 0.04 em), express all insets in em, and snap horizontal/vertical rules to the device pixel grid (round(y \* dpiScale) + 0.5 in device space, or use D2D1\_ANTIALIAS\_MODE\_ALIASED for axis-aligned rules) during Draw using the target DPI; delimiters should become glyphs (see math-layout-07).
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Narrow the rationale. Cambria Math's MATH table (read from the installed cambria.ttc, font 1, UPM 2048) gives FractionRuleThickness, OverbarRuleThickness, UnderbarRuleThickness and RadicalRuleThickness all equal to 133 (0.065 em). So one shared rule width matches the font, and 0.05 em is thinner than the font's rules, not 'heavy'. At typical sizes (16-px body gives an 18.4-DIP math font) the 1-DIP clamp applies; Cambria would give about 1.2 DIP. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None. No test checks math stroke width, rule position or pixel coverage.

### math-layout-17

**Font is hard-coded to Cambria Math / en-US with no fallback detection; \\text uses the math font and \\mathbf/\\mathit rely on DWrite simulations**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:318` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.11

- **What**: Advance creates every format with `CreateTextFormat(L"Cambria Math", nullptr, bold ? BOLD : NORMAL, italic ? ITALIC : NORMAL, ..., L"en-US", ...)` (318-325), ignoring LayoutOptions.fontFamily/locale. If Cambria Math is unavailable (server/container images, font-restricted builds, non-Windows renderers) DirectWrite silently substitutes the default family: \\mathbb/\\mathcal code points (NativeMath.cpp 38-68) then come from system fallback and there is no diagnostic. `\text{...}` is shaped in Cambria Math although TeX sets it in the surrounding text font. \\mathbf and \\mathit request weight/style on a single-face font, so DWrite synthesises bold/oblique instead of using the Mathematical Bold/Italic alphanumerics that Cambria Math contains and that Alphabet() already uses for \\mathbb.
- **Failure**: On a machine without Cambria Math, `$\mathbb{R}$` renders from whatever fallback has U+211D and fractions/delimiters are measured against Segoe UI's metrics without any UnsupportedMath diagnostic; `\text{speed in m/s}` next to Segoe UI body text is visibly in Cambria; `$\mathit{x}$` is an obliqued upright x rather than the italic design.
- **Fix**: Resolve the math family once per preparation with IDWriteFontCollection::FindFamilyName over a documented list (Cambria Math, STIX Two Math, Latin Modern Math, XITS Math) and check for a MATH table; on failure either reject the formula with a diagnostic or use a documented degraded mode; pass options.locale; shape \\text in options.fontFamily; map \\mathbf/\\mathit/\\mathsf/\\mathtt via Alphabet() to the Mathematical Alphanumeric blocks.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: \$\\mathit{x}\$ and \$\\mathbf{v}\$ render with DirectWrite-synthesised oblique/bold of the upright Cambria Math face instead of true italic/bold designs. On an image without Cambria Math (for example Server Core), every formula silently shapes through system font fallback with no diagnostic and is measured against the fallback font's metrics. The locale is fixed at en-US regardless of LayoutOptions.locale. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None. TestRichMathAlignedCasesAndMatrixGeometry (Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:15-44) uses the system Cambria Math implicitly and checks only column x-alignment. No test covers font availability, fallback or style synthesis.

### math-layout-20

**Measure is recomputed inside Place (twice per matrix cell) and Leaf() is a linear search; node-count lambdas are triplicated**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.PreparedMath.h:191` · simplification · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.11

- **What**: Place() calls `Measure(node, size)` for every non-leaf node (191) and then Measure again for children (201, 210, 223, 259, 266, 273, 298); Measure(Matrix) measures each cell in both the column pass (92-93) and the row pass (101), and Place(Matrix) has three near-identical per-cell loops (257-259, 264-269, 271-277) each re-measuring. Measure(Text) resolves the leaf with `std::ranges::find(glyphs, &node, &Glyph::node)` (55-61), O(glyphs) per call. For a 3-level nest of 5-cell matrices this is thousands of Measure calls per reflow (bounded by the depth-24/512-node caps, so not a stall, but pure waste on the owner thread). [...]
- **Failure**: Before: Place(root) for `\begin{pmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} a&b \\ c&d \end{pmatrix} & 0 \\ 0 & 1 \end{pmatrix}` measures the inner matrix cells ~40 times and each leaf lookup scans the glyph vector. After: Gather assigns each node a pre-order index and stores it on Glyph; one Measure pass fills `std::vector<Extent> extents` indexed by that order; Place reads `extents[index]`; matrix column widths/row heights are computed once into two small vectors used by both passes. MathExpression gains a `nodeCount`/`depth` computed once by the parser and reused by the semantic builder.
- **Fix**: Cache extents per node in a vector built during one Measure pass (indices assigned in Gather), make Place read-only with respect to measurement, factor the matrix column/row metrics into a shared helper, store the leaf index on Glyph, and expose MathExpression::NodeCount() from the parser.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Final PreparedMath::Advance for nested matrices re-measures each inner subtree many times; the nested 2x2-in-2x2 pmatrix measures inner cells more than 100 times, each Text measure doing a linear glyph scan. The work is bounded by the 512-node and 24-depth caps, so it is owner-thread waste, not a hang. Node counting has two recursive implementations (NativeMath.cpp:440-449 with depth, RichSemanticDocument.cpp:321-328 without) plus the Make counter. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None measures preparation cost or Measure call counts for nested math.

### math-layout-21

**Math placements are smuggled through NativeImageGeometry and inherit image-only invariants (raster target identity, pixel dedup, diagram brushes)**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:92` · architecture · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.5

- **What**: NativeImagePlacement holds both `pixels/bitmap` and `std::unique_ptr<PreparedMath> math` with Size()/Baseline() branching on `math` (92-110). Consequences: the NativeImageGeometry ctor throws `"Image raster preparation requires the owner's target"` for a math-only document even though math never uploads a bitmap (117-118, constructed whenever Maths() is non-empty at 289-293); Draw refuses any target other than `targetIdentity` for math-only state although DrawTextLayout/DrawLine are target-agnostic (1670-1671); PrepareOne's shared-pixel search iterates over math entries with null pixels (156-158); HasNativeObjects() is true for math-only documents (1355-1358) so the editor allocates opaque diagram fill brushes it never uses (DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp 1400-1408); [...]
- **Failure**: Any future host that prepares semantic geometry without a raster target (print preview, thumbnail, worker-side measurement) or that draws the same prepared math into a second compatible target hits an invalid\_argument/logic\_error purely because the formula sits in the image list; adding a third inline object type (e.g. inline SVG) would require more `if (math)` branches in Size/Baseline/PrepareOne/Draw/Placed.
- **Fix**: Introduce a NativeInlineObject record (`std::variant<ImagePlacement, MathPlacement>` or a separate `NativeMathGeometry` with its own phase) sharing only the display range/bounds/owner fields consumed by SemanticBox::Caret, RangeFromState and Draw; make the raster-target requirement and target-identity check apply only when an image placement exists; have HasNativeObjects distinguish diagrams (which need fill brushes) from inline objects.

If internal code ever calls PreparedSemanticGeometry::Begin / RichEditorLayout::BeginPreparation with raster == nullptr (the default argument) for a document that has math but no images, preparation throws invalid\_argument ("Image raster preparation requires the owner's target"), although math needs no raster target. [...]
- **Existing tests**: No test prepares a math-only document without a raster target or draws one into a second target. Scope.Tests.NativeDevicePolicy.h:33-61 covers math in an editor across device replacement (the resource generation advances), and Scope.Tests.RichSemanticEditor.h:254-287 (MathAndBroaderDiagrams) covers [...]

### math-layout-22

**Caret beside a tall math object is anchored to the object's bottom, not the text baseline**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:196` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.12

- **What**: SemanticBox::Caret, for a caret at an image/math object's display edge, sets `caret.height = std::min(image.caretHeight, image.bounds.bottom - image.bounds.top); caret.y = image.bounds.bottom - caret.height;` (202-203) with caretHeight = options.fontSizeDip (828). For math the object's bottom is baseline + extent.below (≈1.15 x size for a fraction, see math-layout-06), so the caret sits roughly one em lower than the caret at the neighbouring text position.
- **Failure**: In `Before $\frac{a}{b}$ after`, pressing Right from the end of 'Before ' moves the caret to the object edge where it jumps ~20 DIP down and then back up when it reaches 'after'; IME candidate windows anchored to the caret follow the jump.
- **Fix**: For math placements compute caret.y from the object's baseline: `bounds.top + Baseline()*scale - lineAscent` with height = line ascent + descent (from the owning PreparedLayout line metrics), keeping the bottom-anchored rule for bitmaps only.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Source `Before $\frac{a}{b}$ after` at 16 DIP body text, with the math font at 18.4. Carets at text positions on that line (for example 'Befor\|e') span the full tall line cell, from line top to line bottom. The caret immediately before the opening `$` (display.first, the end of 'Before ') and the caret after the closing `$` (display.last) are each 16 DIP tall and end at baseline + extent.below\*scale. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None pins the vertical position of the caret at a math edge. Scope.Tests.SemanticDiagrams.h:168 checks only that diagram source-caret height is at most fontSizeDip. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticEditor.h:254-287 checks math object bounds and source round-trips, not caret geometry.

### mermaid-01

**State/class descriptions, transition labels and aliases bypass Label() validation and silently render unsupported markup**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeFlowchart.cpp:294` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.7

- **What**: Flowchart node/edge labels go through Label() (lines 69-120), which rejects `<`, `>`, `&`, backtick, `#`, `$$`, `fa:` icon prefixes as UnsupportedLabelMarkup, rejects control characters as UnexpectedToken and decodes `\n`/`\"`/`\\`. The three label paths added for the state/class profile do none of that: the node description branch (lines 290-298) does `label.append(L"\n").append(Text().substr(at, end - at))` after only a size check; the transition/association label branch (lines 342-350) does `edge.label.assign(Text().substr(at, end - at))`; and the `state "..." as` alias scan (lines 257-264) copies everything up to the first `"` with no escape or content validation (and reports an oversize alias as MissingLabelClose rather than LabelCapacity). [...]
- **Failure**: '''mermaid\\nclassDiagram\\nList \<\|-- Stack\\nStack : +push(T~int~ item)\\n''' parses as Prepared and the node is painted with the literal text '+push(T~int~ item)', whereas Mermaid renders the generic as 'T\<int>'; the spec (UI\_ControlsAndLayout.md:1739-1741) says class generics remain exact fenced source and :1403-1404 says unsupported label syntax MUST NOT silently acquire a different label meaning. Likewise 'stateDiagram-v2\\nA --> B : go &amp; [...]
- **Fix**: Factor the body of Label()'s per-unit acceptance (markup rejection, control-character rejection, escape decoding, capacity) into a shared `PlainText(at, end, value)` helper and use it for the `:` description, `:` transition label and `state "..." as` alias; additionally reject `~` in class members (generic marker) with UnsupportedLabelMarkup; report alias overflow as LabelCapacity. Add scenarios for `T~int~`, `<b>`, `&amp;`, `#35;`, control characters and `\n` in all three paths.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Rendered as a Prepared diagram with literal text and no diagnostic: - ```mermaid\nclassDiagram\nList <|-- Stack\nStack : +push(T~int~ item)\n``` paints `+push(T~int~ item)` (generic marker shown as-is). - `stateDiagram-v2\nA --> B : go <br/> stop` and `A : <b>x</b>` paint the tags literally. - `state "<i>x</i>" as S` paints the tags literally. - `A --> B : a &amp b` (no semicolon) paints the entity literally. - `A : x\u0001y` passes a C0 control to CreateTextLayout, while `graph TD;A[x\u0001y]` [...]
- **Existing tests**: No test covers markup, generics, entities, control characters or alias capacity on the state/class label paths. Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:86-87, Scope.Tests.RichSemanticEditor.h:257-258 and Scope.Tests.MarkdownBlocks.h:390-392 cover only plain descriptions and labels.

### mermaid-02

**Every width-only reflow throws away all diagram label layouts and path geometries; PreparedFlowchart's width-independent coalescing is unreachable in production**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:1098` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W3.5

- **What**: PreparedFlowchart::Begin deliberately ignores widthDip/codeFont/alignment (SameTypography, PreparedFlowchart.cpp:17-20, 75-83, 181-184) so that a graph whose labels are already shaped returns Complete on a reflow. But PreparedSemanticGeometry builds a brand-new State for every Begin that fails SameOptions (which includes widthDip, line 221-225), State's constructor creates a fresh NativeDiagramGeometry whose placements have null `paint` (lines 294-303), and Advance does `placement.paint = std::make_unique<PreparedFlowchart>()` whenever `!placement.paint` (lines 1096-1102). Nothing is ever transferred from `state_->native`. The only callers of the coalescing path are tests (Scope.Tests.PreparedFlowchart.h:72-74).
- **Failure**: A document containing one 100-node, 60-labelled-edge diagram; the user drags the window edge. Each intermediate width produces a new semantic geometry State, which recreates the IDWriteTextFormat, 160 IDWriteTextLayouts and up to 60 arrow + diamond ID2D1PathGeometry objects for that diagram. RichTextEditor advances with the default LayoutWorkBudget{4, 4096} (RichTextEditor.cpp:1007, RichTextLayout.h:134), so the diagram alone consumes at least 40 owner turns before its paragraph can be positioned at the new width, on every resize step; [...]
- **Fix**: When creating the next Preparation/State, move `placements[i].paint` from `state_->native` (or `next_->state->native`) for diagrams whose `graph` shared\_ptr, drawing factory and epochs match, then call `paint->Begin(...)` which returns Complete without SDK work; alternatively keep a per-PreparedSemanticGeometry `std::vector<std::unique_ptr<PreparedFlowchart>>` keyed by graph pointer that outlives States. Add a test asserting zero CreateTextLayout calls for a width-only `Begin` on a document with a diagram (CountingWriteFactory already exists).
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Every width-only reflow (for example, dragging the window edge) of a document containing a native diagram rebuilds that diagram's text format, all label layouts and its arrow/diamond path geometries from scratch. For a diagram with 100 nodes and 60 labelled edges, that is about 160 layouts, or at least 40 owner Advance turns at the default {4, 4096} budget. The previous geometry stays visible until the new one commits. Nothing is incorrect, and the latency cost has not been measured.
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.PreparedFlowchart.h:67-74 checks coalescing on a single PreparedFlowchart instance only. No test checks the SDK call count for a width-only PreparedSemanticGeometry::Begin on a document with a diagram.

### mermaid-03

**Router gives every edge its own exterior lane and per-rank corridor row, so ordinary diagrams become comb-shaped and overflow the view width**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeFlowchartLayout.cpp:357` · architecture · minor · disputed (refute: refuted; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C6

- **What**: Routes() always emits the same six-point path: down from the source into a corridor row below its rank, horizontally to an exterior lane right (forward/self) or left (back) of the whole band, vertically to a corridor row above the target rank, back to the target. Ports() allocates one corridor row per edge endpoint per rank (`++before[rank]`, `++after[rank]`) and one lane per edge (`leftLanes++`/`rightLanes++`); RowPositions() then adds `before[row]*PortGap` and `after[row]*PortGap` (16 DIP each) to every rank height. Even an edge to the node directly below it travels to the band edge and back. The contract (UI\_ControlsAndLayout.md:1426-1429) mandates distinct exterior lanes, so this is by design, but the design makes the feature unusable beyond toy graphs.
- **Failure**: `graph TD;A-->B` is 64x136 DIP (test oracle, Scope.Tests.FlowchartLayout.h:54-63) where a straight connector needs 56x104. `graph TD;A-->B1;...;A-->B100`: after[0]=100 and before[1]=100 give 1600-DIP corridors below rank 0 and above rank 1, 100 right lanes add 1600 DIP of empty width; the diagram is ~3304 DIP tall and ~8000 DIP wide (row 1 alone is 6376 DIP), so RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:750 throws NativeObjectCapacity("Diagram does not fit this view width") and the user sees fenced source for a graph Mermaid draws in two rows. [...]
- **Fix**: Plan item: (a) route forward edges between adjacent ranks whose source/target x-ranges overlap as a straight or single-bend connector inside RankGap with no corridor rows; (b) share corridor rows per rank and assign a distinct offset only to edges that actually conflict; (c) keep exterior lanes for back/self/skip-rank edges and order nodes within a rank by median of predecessors to reduce crossings; (d) update the spec clause and the hand-calculated oracles. Keep determinism and the no-allocation-while-advancing rule (fixed arrays already exist).
- **Verifier (refute, refuted)**: The mechanics are described accurately: - Ports() gives one corridor row per endpoint per rank and one lane per edge (NativeFlowchartLayout.cpp:265-278). - RowPositions adds before/after × PortGap (308-309). - Routes always emits the six-point exterior-lane path (357-360), with Margin 8, RankGap 24 and PortGap 16 (line 11). The finding itself says this is "by design", and the owning contract requires it. UI\_ControlsAndLayout.md:1426-1429: "Every edge gets a distinct exterior lane; [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.FlowchartLayout.h:48-75 pins the 64x136 two-node extent, the six-point corridor path and the separate lanes for parallel edges, cycles and self-loops as the intended behaviour.

`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeFlowchart.cpp:147` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.7

### mermaid-08

**One wide edge label widens every lane: laneWidth is a single global maximum**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeFlowchartLayout.cpp:179` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: C6

- **What**: Adjacency() computes `laneWidth = std::max(laneWidth, Cross(edgeLabels[index]) + Margin)` once for the whole graph; Routes() places lane i at `cross = ... + lane[index] * laneWidth`, and Rows() rejects the diagram when `bandLeft + bandWidth + rightLanes * laneWidth + 2*Margin > MaximumExtent`. Lanes that carry no label therefore reserve the width of the widest label in the graph.
- **Failure**: A graph with 300 unlabelled forward edges and one edge labelled with 220-DIP text: laneWidth becomes 228, rightLanes\*laneWidth = 68,400 > 65,536, so Rows() publishes FlowchartLayoutIssue::DiagramExtent and the editor falls back to fenced source, although per-lane widths would need 299\*16 + 228 = 5,012 DIP. Below the cap the same graph is painted 14x wider than necessary (every empty lane is 228 DIP).
- **Fix**: Store per-lane widths (`std::array<double, Edges> laneWidthLeft/Right`) in Ports(), accumulate prefix offsets in Rows(), and use `laneOffset[lane[index]]` in Routes(); keep the 16-DIP minimum for unlabelled lanes.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: A TD graph with 300 unlabelled forward edges and one 220-DIP-wide edge label gets laneWidth 228, and 301\*228 = 68,628 > 65,536, so Rows() publishes DiagramExtent and the editor keeps fenced source, although per-lane widths would need about 5 kDIP. More commonly, one long label inflates every lane to that label's width (for example, 10 edges plus one 150-DIP label give a 1,580-DIP lane band instead of about 302), and the painted diagram is many times wider than necessary. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.FlowchartLayout.h:48-86 (unlabelled geometry oracle with laneWidth 16), :175-176 (labelled invariants only), :261-266 (huge-label DiagramExtent). None pins or contradicts uniform lane width.
