### mermaid-15

**Layout state machine mixes three dispatch styles: Rank() multiplexes three stages and two stages are inlined in Step()**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeFlowchartLayout.cpp:218` · simplification · minor · confirmed (feasibility: confirmed) · plan: W2.11

- **What**: Every stage of NativeFlowchartLayout::Preparation is a one-stage method except Rank(), which branches on `stage == Indegree / ChooseRank / RankEdges` internally (218-264), and NormalizeNodes/NormalizeEdges, which are written inline inside Step()'s switch (427-449) using the same cursor/Then pattern as the methods. Stage::Finish only sets `done`. This makes the per-event accounting ("an event handles one node, edge, DFS transition or rank") harder to audit than necessary.
- **Failure**: Before: a reader verifying the documented event budget must trace Rank()'s three paths and two inline cases. After: `Indegree()`, `ChooseRank()`, `RankEdges()`, `NormalizeNodes()`, `NormalizeEdges()` each 8-15 lines, and Step() is a pure dispatch table.
- **Fix**: Split Rank() into three methods and move the two normalize cases into methods; no behaviour change, verify with the existing one-event vs batched determinism test.
- **Verifier (feasibility, confirmed)**: Pure refactor: add Indegree(), ChooseRank() (keep the logic\_error invariant and the full scan of up to 256 nodes per event), RankEdges(), NormalizeNodes() and NormalizeEdges(), and make Step() a one-method-per-stage dispatch. Verify with the existing one-event vs batched(113) determinism checks, the hand-computed two-rank oracle, and the 256-node maximum-depth chain. ## Images, paths and clipboard (17)

### images-clipboard-01

**Scheme-relative Markdown destination //host/share/x.png becomes a UNC file request**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichImagePaths.h:257` · security · major · refuted (refute: refuted; reproduce: refuted) · plan: not scheduled (refuted)

- **What**: In ResolveLocalImagePath the non-`file:` branch only rejects destinations that contain a colon (`const auto colon = destination.find(L':'); if (colon != npos && !(colon == 1u && ...)) return nullopt; raw.assign(destination);`). A destination that starts with `//` has no colon, so it reaches DecodeLocalImageUriPath and then ParseLocalFilePath, which does `std::ranges::replace(result.text, L'/', L'\\')` (line 75) and accepts `\\server\share\...` as a UNC root (lines 94-110). Per RFC 3986 and every Markdown/HTML renderer, `//host/path` is a network-path reference (scheme-relative http/https), i.e. a remote resource, which the contract says MUST NOT become a file request (Specs/UI/UI\_ControlsAndLayout.md:843 'malformed/remote URI schemes MUST NOT become file requests'). [...]
- **Failure**: A user opens a Markdown document pasted from the web containing `![logo] (//cdn.attacker.example/x/logo.png)`. RichImageResources::Advance resolves it to `\\cdn.attacker.example\x\logo.png` and TakePendingImageRequests hands that path to the application. Any application that enabled network files (RichImageLoader::Initialize(..., allowNetworkFiles=true), the documented way to support UNC documents) performs an SMB open to an attacker-chosen host on document open, leaking NTLM credentials / enabling DNS beaconing. [...]
- **Fix**: In the non-`file:` branch return std::nullopt when `destination` starts with `//` (and arguably when it starts with `/`, which is already refused later for relative joins). Keep UNC resolution only through `file://server/share/...` and the backslash spelling. Add `L"//server/share/a.png"` to the rejection vector list and a positive `\\\\server\\share\\a.png` case so the intended spelling stays covered.
- **Verifier (refute, refuted)**: The mechanics are right: in ResolveLocalImagePath (DxUi.RichImagePaths.h:255-261), `//host/share/x.png` has no colon, so raw = destination. DecodeLocalImageUriPath accepts it. ParseLocalFilePath then replaces '/' with '\\' (line 75) and parses `\\host\share\x.png` as a UNC root (lines 94-110). RichImageResources::Advance (line 96) queues it with no other filter. The SemanticBuilder (RichSemanticDocument.cpp:371-372) also adds every image without filtering. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichImagePaths.h:54 pins attacker-selectable UNC resolution via file://server/share as intended; :68 rejects only the percent-encoded `%2f%2f` spelling as an encoded-separator case; there is no case for raw `//server/share/a.png`.

- **Fix**: Rewrite the intro paragraph to say images display caller-serviced native pixels (built-in PNG/JPEG/BMP/GIF/TIFF first frame) with alt text as fallback, link to the 'Native image rendering' section, and state only the open M5g.3 gates.

### images-clipboard-03

**RichImageResources::Take has a dead guard and a redundant clear/swap pattern**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichImageResources.h:156` · simplification · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: Take() first returns early when `!document_ || next_ || none_of(pending && !dispatched)` (line 150), then re-tests `if (document_ && ! next_)` at line 156, which is always true at that point. The function also allocates `staged`, swaps it into `requests`, and separately marks dispatched. Before: 20 lines with two scans of entries\_ plus a dead condition. After: one early-return guard, one loop that both pushes and marks dispatched after a reserve() of the exact pending count, then swap.
- **Failure**: No runtime failure; reviewers must reason about a condition that cannot be false, and the two-phase structure obscures the strong-guarantee intent ('No fallible work follows publication').
- **Fix**: Remove the inner `if (document_ && ! next_)`; count pending-undispatched once, `staged.reserve(count)`, push, swap, then set dispatched. Keep the noexcept tail after the swap.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: No runtime failure. The inner `if (document_ && ! next_)` at line 156 can never be false after the early return at line 150, so it is dead code that obscures the strong-guarantee structure.
- **Existing tests**: None specific; the existing TakePendingImageRequests tests (e.g. Scope.Tests.RichImagePaths.h:162-164) pass with or without the dead condition.

### images-clipboard-04

**Per-file clipboard validation and Markdown label-escape predicate are duplicated**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp:297` · simplification · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: TryPrepareClipboardPaste validates every file path at lines 268-275 (`file.empty() || ! IsValidUtf16(file) || file.find(L'\0') != npos || file.size() > MaximumFileUnits` -> throw invalid\_argument) and repeats the identical check at lines 299-300 inside the literal-fallback loop, even though the first loop already covered every element. In DxUi.RichImagePaths.h BuildMarkdownFileReference the escape predicate `c >= 33 && c <= 126 && !((a-z)||(A-Z)||(0-9))` is written twice (line 418 for counting, line 429 for emitting).
- **Failure**: No runtime defect today; the duplicated predicates can drift (e.g. a future decision to stop escaping '.' in one copy only) and the second validation loop is dead work on the fallback path.
- **Fix**: Hoist a `static bool NeedsMarkdownEscape(wchar_t)` helper in RichImagePaths.h and use it for both the count and emit loops; in TryPrepareClipboardPaste validate once up front and let both branches rely on it.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: No runtime defect today. The second per-file validation at RichTextEditor.cpp:299-300 is unreachable dead work. The two copies of the Markdown escape predicate at RichImagePaths.h:418/429 can drift apart, making the capacity precount disagree with the emitted output.
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichImagePaths.h:172-185 cover the literal-fallback and malformed-path throw (the throw comes from the first loop); escape output is pinned by vectors at :20, :103, :111.

### images-clipboard-05

**A clipboard path with 512+ components throws past the documented quoted-literal fallback; native paste is silently dropped**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichImagePaths.h:139` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.9

- **What**: ParseLocalFilePath throws `std::length_error("image path component capacity exceeded")` when a path has >= 512 components. BuildMarkdownFileReference (line 390) and therefore RichTextEditor::TryPrepareClipboardPaste (DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp:258/279) let that exception propagate instead of returning std::nullopt. The documented/contracted behavior for an unrepresentable structured path is to keep the complete list as quoted literal source (docs/rich-text.md:182-183; Specs/UI/UI\_ControlsAndLayout.md:808-809 'If any structured path is unrepresentable, the complete original list MUST remain quoted source'). On the native route OnKeyDown is noexcept and ends in `catch (const std::exception&) { return false; }` (lines 1957-1960), so the paste does nothing. [...]
- **Failure**: Explorer file list containing `C:\a\a\...\a\photo.png` with 512 directory levels (about 1,100 UTF-16 units, well under the 32,767-unit and 65,536-total limits, valid on a long-path-enabled volume). Ctrl+V in a prepared Rich editor: expected `"C:\a\...\photo.png"` literal paste; actual: nothing is inserted and no error is surfaced (native) or TSF paste fails (embedded). Also reachable through plain text paste of such a path.
- **Fix**: Treat the component cap as a lexical rejection: `return false` from ParseLocalFilePath (callers already handle false), so BuildMarkdownFileReference yields nullopt and the quoted fallback applies; keep the throw only for ResolveLocalImagePath if a distinct diagnostic is wanted (Advance already catches it). Document the 512-component bound in docs/rich-text.md and add a paste test with 512 components expecting the quoted literal.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Prepared Rich editor, CF\_HDROP or CF\_UNICODETEXT holding C:\\a\\a\\...\\a\\photo.png with 512 directory levels plus the file name, which is 513 components and about 1,035 UTF-16 units. Native Ctrl+V: TryPrepareClipboardPaste throws length\_error and OnKeyDown returns false. Nothing is inserted: no Markdown, no quoted literal and, for the text route, no plain-text paste. Embedded TSF paste returns HRESULT\_FROM\_WIN32(ERROR\_BUFFER\_OVERFLOW) and inserts nothing. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None for the component cap. Scope.Tests.RichImagePaths.h:82-89 and :122-129 pin only length\_error from BuildMarkdownFileReference when encoded output exceeds Document::MaximumUnits (22000 CJK units). [...]

### images-clipboard-06

**ClipboardCalls() references the diagnostics-only seam without the DXUI\_ENABLE\_DIAGNOSTICS guard**  
`src/Controls/TextClipboard.cpp:14` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.9

- **What**: `return testClipboardSystemCalls ? *testClipboardSystemCalls : calls;` is compiled unconditionally, while `testClipboardSystemCalls` is declared only under `#if DXUI_ENABLE_DIAGNOSTICS` (TextClipboard.h:12-26) and defined under the same guard (TextClipboard.cpp:114-117). The sibling seams are guarded correctly: FileCalls() in DxUi.RichImage.cpp:39-47 and ActiveTextClipboard() in TextClipboard.cpp:233-240. Configuration.h pins the macro to 1 ('not a consumer-selectable build option'), so this compiles today but is a latent build break and an inconsistency between the three seams.

- **Fix**: Wrap the override test in `#if DXUI_ENABLE_DIAGNOSTICS` exactly like FileCalls(), or drop the guard from the header/definition if the seam is meant to be always-on.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Only editing include/DxUi/Configuration.h to define DXUI\_ENABLE\_DIAGNOSTICS 0 breaks the library build. TextClipboard.cpp then fails on the undeclared type ClipboardSystemCalls (lines 11, 13 and every WindowsTextClipboard use of calls) and on testClipboardSystemCalls. A consumer pre-definition does not reach this code. No supported configuration (x64/ARM64 Debug/Release/ASan) is affected.
- **Existing tests**: None. No build or test runs with DXUI\_ENABLE\_DIAGNOSTICS=0; Configuration.h fixes it at 1.

### images-clipboard-07

**JPEG EXIF orientation query path (/app1/ifd/{ushort=274}) is never exercised by tests**  
`Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichImage.h:253` · test-gap · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.9

- **What**: The decoder selects a different metadata query string for JPEG than for TIFF (DxUi.RichImage.cpp:256: `*clsid == CLSID_WICJpegDecoder ? L"/app1/ifd/{ushort=274}" : L"/ifd/{ushort=274}"`). The test fixture Encode() only writes orientation metadata for TIFF (lines 48-56, `if (container == GUID_ContainerFormatTiff) ... SetMetadataByName(L"/ifd/{ushort=274}", ...)`), the eight-orientation loop uses TIFF only (lines 236-244), and the single JPEG case (lines 253-259) uses the default orientation 1. docs/rich-text.md:230-231 and the contract (UI\_ControlsAndLayout.md:833) promise JPEG EXIF orientation.
- **Failure**: A typo or WIC path change in the JPEG query string (for example `/app1/ifd/exif/{ushort=274}`) would silently make every rotated phone photo render sideways while all suites stay green.
- **Fix**: Extend Encode() to write `/app1/ifd/{ushort=274}` for GUID\_ContainerFormatJpeg (WIC's JPEG encoder supports the APP1 writer) and run the same eight-orientation oracle against a JPEG fixture with distinct solid quadrants (tolerance-based comparison like the existing JPEG check).

Every EXIF-rotated JPEG then decodes with orientation 1, and Scope.Tests.RichImage Core still passes, because its JPEG fixture carries no orientation tag and is a uniform solid colour.
- **Existing tests**: Scope.Tests.RichImage.h:236-244 (TIFF-only eight-orientation oracle) and :253-259 (JPEG dimensions and colour tolerance with no orientation metadata). Neither exercises the /app1/ifd path with a non-default value.

### images-clipboard-08

**RichImageLoader duplicates the results compaction/accounting block in DiscardCancelled and Drain**  
`Samples/EmbeddedControls/RichImageLoader.h:146` · simplification · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: DiscardCancelled (lines 119-133) and Drain(span) (lines 146-160) each: shift surviving results to the front, reset the vacated tail to `{}`, zero `resultBytes`, re-sum `image->Pixels().size()` over survivors, store `hasResults`, and call Schedule. The only difference is the survivor predicate (not cancelled vs. index >= count). Before: ~30 lines of near-identical index arithmetic in two places; after: one `State::CompactResults(Predicate)` that returns the retained count, called from both.
- **Failure**: Maintenance hazard only: a future fix to the accounting (for example excluding images shared between results from resultBytes) must be applied twice; today both copies agree.
- **Fix**: Add a private `template <class Keep> std::size_t Compact(Keep keep) noexcept` on State that performs shift/zero/re-sum/hasResults under the already-held lock; DiscardCancelled passes `!request.Cancelled()`, Drain copies out the first `count` then passes an index predicate.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Maintenance hazard only. A future change to resultBytes accounting or the hasResults/Schedule wake policy has to be made in both DiscardCancelled and Drain, and the copies could drift apart. There is no current wrong output.
- **Existing tests**: No test pins the duplication. The loader's wake and accounting behaviour is described in docs/rich-text.md:219-227 and exercised through the sample and worker tests.

### images-clipboard-09

**Decode pipeline makes up to four 16 MiB transient copies per image (owned stream copy, unconditional Orient copy)**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichImage.cpp:230` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: LoadFile reads the file into `std::vector<std::byte> bytes` (line 341) and calls Decode(span). Decode then copies the whole encoded buffer again into `std::vector<BYTE> owned` (lines 230-231, comment 'Retain the stream's backing memory until all lazy WIC sources retire') although every WIC object is a local com\_ptr released before Decode returns, so the caller's span already outlives all lazy sources (the same borrow rule the header states for `cancellation`). After CopyPixels into `pixels` (line 266), `Orient` is called unconditionally (line 272) and always allocates a second full-size RichImage and copies pixel-by-pixel with a per-pixel 4-byte memcpy loop (lines 171-208), even for orientation 1 (the overwhelmingly common case and the only case for PNG/BMP/GIF). [...]
- **Failure**: A document with a 4096x1024 PNG: worker peak RSS rises by ~64 MiB per decode and the orientation-1 copy loop executes 4,194,304 memcpy(4) calls on top of WIC's own conversion; with the 32-source cap and a 32-slot result queue the sample can hold 32 x 16 MiB results plus transient 64 MiB.
- **Fix**: Add a private `RichImage(unsigned, unsigned, std::vector<std::byte>&&)` and have Orient move `pixels` into the output when orientation == 1 (CreatePbgra still needs one copy from its const span). Add an internal `Decode(IWICImagingFactory*, std::vector<BYTE>&& owned, ...)` used by LoadFile so the file buffer feeds the WIC stream directly; keep the copying public Decode for caller-owned const spans or document that the span is borrowed for the call and use a const\_cast with a comment (WIC never writes the buffer).
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: On the worker path (not paint or composition), peak transient memory per decode is 2×encoded + 2×decoded bytes: LoadFile `bytes`, Decode `owned`, `pixels`, and Orient's `staged`. The caps bound that at 64 MiB, including for GIF, about 32 MiB more than needed. For a typical few-MiB 4096x1024 PNG the peak is roughly 40 MiB instead of about 20 MiB. On top of WIC's conversion, there is a redundant 4,194,304-iteration copy loop for orientation 1. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichImage.h covers decode correctness: orientation oracle at 236-243, GIF canvas at 284-359, cancellation and capacity at 274-280. Tests/Embedded/Scope.Tests.RichTextResources.h:68 runs an allocation-failure sweep over Decode. [...]

**GPU image bitmaps are re-uploaded on every width/typography geometry rebuild although target and pixels are unchanged**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.RichSemanticGeometry.cpp:292` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13, W3.4

- **What**: PreparedSemanticGeometry::State's constructor always builds a fresh `NativeImageGeometry` (`images = std::make_unique<NativeImageGeometry>(...)`), whose PrepareOne (lines 153-171) calls UploadImageBitmap for each unique `pixels` pointer. The only reuse is the coalescing check at lines 935-947, which requires `SameOptions(state.options, options)` (including widthDip) and the same images/target/epoch, i.e. an identical Begin. A viewport width change (window resize), a font/typography revision or any source edit creates a new State and re-uploads every unique bitmap, one per owner turn (line 1136-1141), even though `targetIdentity` and `imageEpochs` are retained for exactly this comparison and the immutable `RichImage` pointers are shared across snapshots. [...]

- **Fix**: When Begin replaces a ready `state_` whose `images->targetIdentity == raster` and `imageEpochs == epochs`, seed the new NativeImageGeometry's placements with the prior `bitmap` com\_ptrs for matching `pixels` pointers before staging (a lookup over at most 128 entries), and let PrepareOne skip the upload when `placement.bitmap` is already set. Device replacement still invalidates via the resource epoch.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: With N unique decoded images (at most 32 sources and 64 MiB of retained pixels), every viewport width change, typography or resource-generation change, or semantic-document edit creates a new State and calls CreateBitmap again for all N images. That happens one upload per owner turn, before any text layout of the new geometry. The previous complete geometry keeps painting in the meantime. Repeated width changes during a drag restart preparation and repeat the uploads already done.
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Embedded/Scope.Tests.RichImageFailures.h:294-332 (TestRichImageRasterFailureAndBudgets) checks exactly 2 uploads for duplicate placements, no upload on an identical Begin, and at most one upload per turn. [...]

`src/Controls/DxUi.RichImage.cpp:131` · simplification · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

The same happens after SetText, SetViewMode(Source), a source edit or a rebind, until the next stage publishes.

- **Existing tests**: Tests/Support/Scope.Tests.RichImage.h:21-55 and 236-243 cover the eight orientations, written only as VT\_UI2. No test covers a non-standard VARTYPE or a metadata query error.

### images-clipboard-16

**Sample loader's default 'declines UNC access' policy is purely lexical and the guide does not say so**  
`Samples/EmbeddedControls/RichImageLoader.h:231` · doc-mismatch · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: The network policy is `! state.allowNetworkFiles && request.FilePath().starts_with(L"\\\\") ? E_ACCESSDENIED : ...`. Paths are canonical `\\server\share\...` only when the document or destination was spelled as UNC/file://host; a mapped network drive (`Z:\`), a `subst` drive, a directory junction/symlink to a network location, or a DFS-mapped letter all pass and are read over the network by LoadFile (which follows reparse points and opens with the `\\?\` prefix). docs/rich-text.md:220-221 ('Its default policy declines UNC access; an application can explicitly authorize network files') reads as if network access is excluded by default.
- **Failure**: Document saved on `Z:` (a mapped share) with `![x] (a.png)`: the sample loader opens `\\?\Z:\...\a.png` over SMB with allowNetworkFiles == false; a consumer copying the sample's policy as a security boundary gets weaker protection than the guide implies.
- **Fix**: State in docs/rich-text.md and the loader comment that the policy is spelling-based (UNC/file://host only) and that applications needing a true boundary must check the volume (GetDriveTypeW / GetVolumePathNameW / final path via GetFinalPathNameByHandleW) in their own authorization step, which the contract already assigns to the caller.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: The sample loader runs with allowNetworkFiles == false. A document saved on a mapped share (Z:) containing ![x]\(a.png) resolves to Z:\\...\\a.png, passes the starts\_with(L"\\\\\\\\") check and is read over SMB. A consumer who reads docs/rich-text.md's 'authorize network files' as a default network exclusion and copies the sample policy gets only a UNC-spelling filter.
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichTextInput.cpp:219-234 covers default refusal only for UNC-spelled paths. No test or doc covers mapped or subst drives.

`src/Controls/DxUi.RichImageResources.h:111` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13, W3.4

- **Failure**: Document references a 15 MiB TIFF that fails decode (unsupported compression) plus ordinary text. Advance does not reuse the failed entry, because reuse requires `entry.image`. Take() then sends a new request for the same path, and the sample host submits it on the next render. [...]

They are called from `noexcept` ITextRangeProvider methods with no try/catch: CompareEndpoints (5819), ExpandToEnclosingUnit (5955, 5981), GetText (6345), Move (6410, 6425, 6450), MoveEndpointByUnit (6565, 6580, 6609), GetBoundingRectangles (6198, 6257) and the owner-thread executors ExecuteExpandToVisualLineOnWindowThread/ExecuteMoveByVisualLineOnWindowThread/ExecuteMoveEndpointByVisualLineOnWindowThread/ExecuteResolveBoundsOnWindowThread/ExecuteSelectOnWindowThread (6814, 6835, 6862, 6905, 6721). The embedded test harness (Tests/Embedded/Embedded.Tests.Embedded.cpp 47-51) already replaces operator new to throw bad\_alloc after N allocations, so an allocation sweep over these calls would terminate the test process.

Where an allocation is genuinely required (GetText BSTR, visual-line vectors, rect vectors) wrap the method body in a function-try-block returning E\_OUTOFMEMORY, and drop `noexcept` from helpers that allocate (TryResolveTextRangeVisualLineSpans, TryGetEnclosingTextRangeVisualLineSpan, [...]

The copies are ResolveText/ResolveControlText (RichTextEditor::GetText returns a std::wstring). The helpers are the visual-line span vectors and caret-rect vectors. [...]

`src/Controls/DxUi.Accessibility.cpp:7345` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

GetBoundingRectangles copies twice (once through ResolveText on the calling thread, once through RichTextEditor::GetText on the owner thread) and then compares the full text against the record. ### accessibility-03

**GetAttributeValue/FindAttribute scan SourceRuns from index 0 despite an existing lower\_bound helper**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.Accessibility.cpp:6154` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: GetAttributeValue iterates `for (const auto& run : semanticRecord->RichSemantic()->SourceRuns())` from the first run and only breaks once `run.start >= range.end`, so a query on a caret near the end of a run-dense document visits every run before it. FindAttribute (6045) likewise walks all runs from the start even though the match can only begin at or after `range.start`. `SourceFormatIndex` (5860-5866) already provides the O(log n) lower\_bound to the first run overlapping an offset.
- **Failure**: Document with ~5,000 source runs (typical for a long GFM file with inline code/emphasis); Narrator at the end of the document asks FontWeight, IsItalic, StyleId, FontName and FontSize per cursor step -> five full scans of 5,000 runs per step, each comparing SourceAttribute values, instead of one binary search plus a few overlapping runs.
- **Fix**: Start both loops at `runs.begin() + SourceFormatIndex(*record, range.start)` (for the backward FindAttribute keep the same start and iterate to the end, or iterate in reverse from the run containing range.end).
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: A query near the end of a run-dense document scans every preceding run. Each skipped run costs only two integer comparisons over a contiguous span; SourceAttribute is evaluated only for runs that overlap the range. Five attribute queries per step over 5,000 runs cost a few microseconds in total. Starting at SourceFormatIndex(range.start) would remove that linear prefix. Results are correct in either case.
- **Existing tests**: None measures attribute-query cost. Functional attribute and FindAttribute behavior is covered in Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichTextAccessibility.cpp.

Meanwhile the owner thread retires the semantic epoch, for example on a keystroke. The caller's guard passes, Create sees HasCurrentRichSemantic()==false at RichAccessibility.h:118 and returns null without allocating, and the method reports E\_OUTOFMEMORY instead of UIA\_E\_ELEMENTNOTAVAILABLE. [...]

GetRowHeaders and GetRowHeaderItems are identical except for the role compared. CellSpan (670-678) performs the full CellCoordinate lookup and only afterwards checks `result` for null.

After: one `template <class Body> HRESULT WithElement(std::optional<RichText::SemanticRole> requiredRole, Body&& body) const noexcept` that captures, resolves the record and role, and invokes `body(snapshot, record, element)`; each COM method becomes 3-5 lines, and GetRowHeaders/GetRowHeaderItems collapse to `return WithElement(Table/Cell, [&]{ return SetProviderArray(result, {}); });`.
- **Fix**: Introduce the WithElement helper (and a `RoleIs(record, role)` predicate), move the E\_POINTER check first in CellSpan, and keep only method-specific logic in each override.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: About 13 COM methods plus the private helpers TableCount, CellCoordinate and Headers repeat the snapshot/record guard. Nine of them also repeat a role guard. A change to the stale-element HRESULT or a new diagnostics check must be applied at all 15 Record(snapshot) sites. Toggle and Invoke keep their owner-thread dispatch before the shared guard. CellSpan does a redundant snapshot capture before the null-pointer check.
- **Existing tests**: None pins the duplicated structure. Behavioral tests of the semantic Table/Cell/Task providers would be the regression net for a refactor.

### accessibility-08

**Rich font-family read throws wil::ResultException inside a publish path whose embedded caller catches only bad\_alloc**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.Accessibility.cpp:2114` · bug · minor · refuted (refute: refuted; reproduce: refuted) · plan: not scheduled (refuted)

- **What**: In PublishWindowHostAccessibilitySnapshot the rich branch's `readFamily` lambda uses `THROW_HR(E_UNEXPECTED)` when `host.GetTextFormat(...)` returns null and `THROW_IF_FAILED(format->GetFontFamilyName(...))` (2111-2118). These throw `wil::ResultException` (a std::exception, not std::bad\_alloc). The native caller FlushNativeAccessibilitySnapshot catches `std::exception` (12714) and is safe, but the embedded caller `EmbeddedHost::UpdateAccessibility(...) noexcept` (12830) wraps the publish in `try { ... } catch (const std::bad_alloc&)` only (12850-12863). `ControlHost::GetTextFormat` can legitimately return nullptr when `EnsureDeviceIndependentResources()` fails (DxUi.WindowHost.cpp 2346-2353).
- **Failure**: Embedded host with an acknowledged semantic document; DirectWrite device-independent resources are released/recreated and recreation fails (EnsureDeviceIndependentResources returns false) before the application calls UpdateAccessibility. `GetTextFormat(FontRole::Body)` returns nullptr, readFamily executes THROW\_HR(E\_UNEXPECTED), the exception passes the bad\_alloc-only catch and escapes a noexcept function, terminating the process instead of returning an HRESULT and keeping the prior snapshot. [...]
- **Fix**: Treat a missing text format as 'no rich metadata this publish' (skip `record.controlRichText`, keep the source TextPattern) instead of throwing, and/or add `catch (const std::exception&) { return E_FAIL; }` to the embedded publish entry to match the native one.
- **Verifier (refute, refuted)**: The claimed embedded failure path cannot be reached. The throw needs host.GetTextFormat(FontRole::Body or Monospace) to return nullptr, or GetFontFamilyName to fail, while an embedded accessibility target is attached. Embedded hosts never reach that state. (a) The DirectWrite factory comes only from EmbeddedHost::ReplaceDevice (Embedded.cpp:167), and GraphicsDevice::Create guarantees it is non-null (Embedded.cpp:49, RETURN\_IF\_FAILED on DWriteCreateFactory). [...]
- **Existing tests**: No test forces GetTextFormat to return null on an attached embedded host, and none can be written without breaking the invariants cited above.

## Host integration and sample (15)

### host-integration-01

**Embedded clipboard paste rewrites CRLF to LF for RichTextEditor while the native host inserts exact clipboard bytes**  
`src/Controls/TextInputServices.cpp:857` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.8

- **What**: TextInputServices::Clipboard(Paste) unconditionally normalizes the pasted text before committing it: `for (...) { if (character == L'\r') { if (... == L'\n') ++i; character = L'\n'; } if (! multiline && (character == L'\n' || character == L'\t')) character = L' '; ... }` (lines 857-870). The native RichTextEditor paste path (DxUi.RichTextEditor.cpp 1885-1893) does `host.ReadClipboardContent(content)` and then `ReplaceSelection(std::move(content.text))` with no normalization, and Enter inserts `text = L"\r\n"` (line 1776). The rich editor's own file-reference path even derives the document EOL from the source (lines 246-252), but that is bypassed for plain text because the service hands the transform already-normalized `content.text` (line 873). [...]
- **Failure**: Run DxUi.EmbeddedControls --rich-text (document seeded with CRLF, EmbeddedScene.h:36-54). Copy two lines from Notepad (CRLF) and press Ctrl+V: PreTranslate routes to Clipboard(Paste) and the inserted text contains LF only; press Enter afterwards and a CRLF is inserted. The Markdown source now has mixed line endings, undo granularity and later table/list commands use the local EOL (rich-text.md:1066) inconsistently, and the identical paste in a native ControlHost keeps CRLF. Any consumer that round-trips the file sees the embedded host silently changing bytes the user never typed.
- **Fix**: Let the control decide: pass the un-normalized text to TransformPaste and have EmbeddedHost::TransformClipboardPaste return S\_OK with the exact clipboard text when the focused control is a RichTextEditor and TryPrepareClipboardPaste declines (mirroring the native 'V' branch), applying the CRLF/tab normalization only when the transform declines and the snapshot is a TextField-style client; add an embedded test that pastes CRLF text into a RichTextEditor through TextInputServices and asserts byte equality with the native path.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Run the embedded --rich-text sample (CRLF-seeded document), focus the editor and paste multi-line CRLF clipboard text with Ctrl+V. TextInputServices normalizes it to LF and EmbeddedHost::TransformClipboardPaste returns S\_FALSE for non-file-reference text, so LF-only lines are committed into the CRLF document. The same paste into a native ControlHost RichTextEditor inserts CRLF unchanged. Result: the Markdown source differs by host and has mixed line endings.
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Controls/Controls.Tests.TextInputServices.h:851-858 pins normalization for a generic TextField-style multiline client. Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichTextInput.cpp:372-390 covers only file-reference paste through the embedded service. [...]

### host-integration-02

**Hiding, zero-sizing or replacing the device on an EmbeddedHost drops logical keyboard focus; the native host keeps it**  
`src/Rendering/Embedded.cpp:207` · bug · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.8

- **What**: EmbeddedHost::ReleaseSurface() calls `CancelTextInput();` (line 207), and CancelTextInput() does `_host.DeactivateTextInput(); _host.SetFocusControl(nullptr);` (lines 1063-1064). ReleaseSurface runs for SetVisible(false) (line 230), zero-sized Prepare (line 304) and ReplaceDevice (line 159). The native ControlHost handles the same events by cancelling only the text session: WM\_SHOWWINDOW(FALSE) does `CancelPreparedContent(); DeactivateNativeTextInputSession();` (DxUi.WindowHost.cpp:2909-2910) and OnSize zero does `DeactivateNativeTextInputSession()` (4466), both leaving `_focusedControl` intact and reactivating the editor on show/restore (2892-2896, 4479-4483). [...]
- **Failure**: In the sample, minimize the window (WM\_SIZE SIZE\_MINIMIZED -> SetVisible(false), Main.cpp:265-269) while the RichTextEditor has the caret, then restore: the editor has no focus, the caret is gone, DispatchKey/DispatchCharacter are rejected (TextInputIsAvailable needs HasFocus()) and TextInputServices gets no client until the user clicks the editor again. The same happens after a device-loss ReplaceDevice, which the docs describe as preserving the logical model. [...]
- **Fix**: In ReleaseSurface call `_host.DeactivateTextInput()` (cancels composition and the session) instead of CancelTextInput, keep SetFocusControl(nullptr) only in the public CancelTextInput (OS focus loss), and on SetVisible(true)/first sized Prepare/ReplaceDevice re-run `_host.ActivateTextInput(_host.GetFocusControl(), false)` when the retained focus control SupportsTextInput, mirroring WindowHost.cpp:2892-2896; update the Embedded text-input tests to assert focus survives hide/zero/replace and document the behaviour in Embedded.h and hosting.md.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Focus a RichTextEditor or TextField in an EmbeddedHost while the application window keeps OS focus. Then (a) call SetVisible(false) and SetVisible(true), for example on a pane or tab switch, (b) call Prepare(0,h) and then Prepare(w,h), or (c) call ReplaceDevice after device loss and prepare again. In each case GetFocusControl() is null, the caret is gone, ReadTextInput returns S\_FALSE and DispatchCharacter is rejected until the user clicks or tabs back. [...]
- **Existing tests**: Tests/Embedded/Embedded.Tests.EmbeddedTextInput.h:318-342 re-focuses after hide, zero-size and ReplaceDevice without asserting focus either way. Tests/Embedded/Scope.Tests.RichTextResources.h:2372-2393 checks model, selection and history survival but not focus. [...]

### host-integration-03

**Sample refreshes the TSF client on every message, re-copying the whole document ~30 times per WM\_MOUSEMOVE**  
`Samples/EmbeddedControls/Main.cpp:183` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.8

- **What**: Main.cpp installs `const auto refresh = wil::scope_exit([&] { app->RefreshText(); });` for every window message (line 183) and also calls `RefreshText();` at every GetMessage iteration (line 363) and again after PreTranslate (368) and after HandleMessage (180). RefreshText (51-83) does `View().ReadTextInput(snapshot)` and `textServices.NotifyChanged()`. NotifyChanged (TextInputServices.cpp:783-797) calls `client->Read` again and then NotifyTextStoreChanged -> NotifyExternalChangesIfNeeded (DxUi.TextStoreACP.cpp:1864-1871), which calls `target->ReadState` (another client->Read) and `ResolveTextViewportBounds` -> GetScreenRect -> `client->ViewportBounds` (another Read). [...]
- **Failure**: Open --rich-text with a document near the 65,536-unit ceiling and move the mouse across the window: every WM\_MOUSEMOVE triggers two RefreshText passes, each performing ~16 whole-document copies plus UIA diffing, so the UI thread spends its time in string copies while nothing changed; on a slower machine mouse movement becomes visibly sluggish and the hot-input path performs unbounded (per-message) heap work the contracts forbid.
- **Fix**: In the sample call RefreshText only on state transitions (WM\_SETFOCUS/WM\_KILLFOCUS, after an input dispatch that returned true, after Prepare returns S\_OK, after TSF handled a message) instead of in the loop and the scope\_exit; in the library add a revision fast path to ReadTextInput so SyncNativeTextInputSession is skipped when `_state->revision`, the session revision and the editor's Revision() are unchanged since the last sync, and drop the TryReadTextInputState copy by comparing through one ExportTextInputState call.
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Run DxUi.EmbeddedControls --rich-text (or --text-input) with the window and the editor focused, and move the mouse. Each WM\_MOUSEMOVE runs RefreshText twice. Each RefreshText does about four EmbeddedHost::ReadTextInput calls, each copying the source about five times and diffing it, plus extra TSF-side copies. That is roughly 40 or more whole-document allocations and copies per message (about 5 MB of memcpy near the 64Ki-unit ceiling) with no state change. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None. No test or measurement covers per-message TSF refresh cost in the sample or a ReadTextInput fast path.

### host-integration-04

**Content-preparation failures and benign incomplete layouts both surface as E\_ABORT with no wakeup, and the sample treats E\_ABORT as fatal**  
`src/Rendering/Embedded.cpp:395` · architecture · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.8

- **What**: After `if (! _host.PreparePendingPageLayout())`, Prepare returns `E_ABORT` whenever `_contentPreparationContinues` is false (lines 386-396). PreparePendingPageLayout returns a bool that collapses several distinct causes: a control's PrepareContent returned a failure HRESULT (DxUi.Controls.cpp:2310-2313, e.g. E\_OUTOFMEMORY or the E\_UNEXPECTED thrown when GetTextFormat is null at RichTextEditor.cpp:962-963), a child list that grew during the pass (`_pendingPageLayout = true` at 2344-2347), a pending/failed intrinsic layout, or a root replaced during preparation. Before the try block `s.coherent = false` (line 333), so Composite returns S\_FALSE (line 496-497) and the application draws nothing for the view; [...]

- **Fix**: Have PreparePendingPageLayout report an HRESULT (or an enum: Complete, ContinueContent, ContinueLayout, Failed(hr)) so Prepare can return S\_FALSE + wakeup for 'another pass needed' conditions, propagate the control's failure HRESULT for real failures, and reserve E\_ABORT for in-paint tree replacement; fix the sample to re-request preparation on E\_ABORT/S\_FALSE and quit only on device-loss class failures.

A host following docs/layout.md:79 ('the host retries preparation' on E\_ABORT) cannot tell this persistent failure from the documented transient in-paint retirement. [...]

### host-integration-05

**EmbeddedHost::ReadTextInput performs a full side-effecting session sync on every read, including TSF geometry queries**  
`src/Rendering/Embedded.cpp:797` · performance · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W2.13

- **What**: ReadTextInput calls `_host.TryReadTextInputState(control, actual)` (copy of the cached text), then `_host.SyncNativeTextInputSession(control)` (line 799), which re-exports the control state (full GetText() copy), rebuilds `_nativeTextInputStateCache`, raises UIA text/selection events against the previous cache and updates the caret (DxUi.NativeTextInput.cpp:1038-1108), then copies the cache again in TryReadNativeTextInputState. ClientTextStoreTarget::GetScreenRect/GetAcpFromScreenPoint/GetRangeScreenRect/ReadState all route through client->Read (TextInputServices.cpp:133, 184, 307, 330, 353), so every TSF geometry query or external-change check outside a lock pays three whole-document copies plus an accessibility diff even when nothing changed. [...]
- **Failure**: With a 60 KB source and an IME candidate window open, TSF polls GetScreenRect/GetTextExt several times per keystroke; each call triggers ~3 full copies and a UIA diff of the document, multiplying the per-key cost by the document size and generating allocation churn on the input thread for reads that observe no change.
- **Fix**: Record the `_state->revision` and `_nativeTextInputSessionRevision` at the last successful sync in EmbeddedHost::State and skip SyncNativeTextInputSession when both are unchanged (SetText/SetSelection already bump the view revision through RequestInvalidate -> MarkDirty); keep the composing-text comparison but derive it from one ExportTextInputState call instead of a separate TryReadTextInputState copy.

Each TSF GetTextExt under a read lock calls client->RangeBounds. That makes two ReadTextInput calls: one from the client's Read, one inside GetTextInputRangeBounds. Each ReadTextInput copies the whole text about five times and runs an O(n) compare, so one geometry query copies about 10 x 120 KB. [...]
- **Existing tests**: None pins or bounds the per-read cost. Scope.Tests.RichTextInput.cpp:84-88 and 333-337 run the sample EmbeddedTextClient through TextInputServices functionally only.

### host-integration-07

**Prepare unbinds the application's render targets and PS shader resource slot 0 on the immediate context, undocumented**  
`src/Rendering/Embedded.cpp:397` · doc-mismatch · minor · disputed (refute: refuted; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: W1.11

- **What**: Inside Prepare, before drawing into the cached surface, the host does `_host._d3dContext->PSSetShaderResources(0, 1, &empty); _host._d3dContext->OMSetRenderTargets(0, nullptr, nullptr);` on the application's immediate context (lines 397-399). docs/hosting.md documents D3D state changes only for Composite ('It changes D3D state: rebind your state for subsequent drawing', line 34-35) and Rendering\_EmbeddedD3D11.md:51-54 stresses that replacement/detach never touch the caller's bindings; Embedded.h:112 says only that 'Text shaping and raster work happen only here'. The unbinding is reasonable (the surface may still be bound as an SRV from the previous Composite) but is a side effect a host preparing a second view mid-scene must know about.
- **Failure**: A host binds its scene render target, draws background geometry, then notices `NeedsPreparation()` for a second view and calls Prepare before compositing both views: the OMSetRenderTargets(0,...) silently unbinds the scene target and the subsequent application draw calls (before it re-binds for Composite) go nowhere, with no HRESULT or debug-layer error explaining it.
- **Fix**: Document in Embedded.h (Prepare) and hosting.md step 3 that Prepare unbinds OM render targets and PS SRV slot 0 on the immediate context, or narrow the side effect to unbinding only when the view's own SRV is currently bound (save/restore via PSGetShaderResources/OMGetRenderTargets).
- **Verifier (refute, refuted)**: The unbinding at Embedded.cpp:397-399 does happen, but the documented hosting contract rules out the failure scenario. Rendering\_EmbeddedD3D11.md:45 says: 'End preparation before composition and rebind the application's pipeline state.' ### status-truth-06

**\\epsilon/\\varepsilon and \\phi/\\varphi map to the swapped Unicode glyphs**  
`src/Controls/DxUi.NativeMath.cpp:248` · bug · major · confirmed (: confirmed; : confirmed) · plan: W1.6

- **What**: The symbol table maps `epsilon` to U+03B5 (ε) and `varepsilon` to U+03F5 (ϵ) (lines 248-249), and `phi` to U+03C6 (φ) and `varphi` to U+03D5 (ϕ) (lines 265-266). TeX/KaTeX (the pinned reference, ThirdParty/Markdown/README.md:24-26) define `\epsilon` as the lunate U+03F5 and `\varepsilon` as U+03B5, `\phi` as U+03D5 and `\varphi` as U+03C6. Verified by dumping the file's code points (0x03B5 at the `epsilon` entry, 0x03C6 at `phi`).
- **Failure**: Source `$\phi(x)$` renders φ (the varphi form) and `$\epsilon > 0$` renders ε (the varepsilon form); a document that deliberately distinguishes `\phi` from `\varphi` shows the opposite glyphs from every other Markdown/TeX viewer. `theta`/`vartheta` (lines 252-253) are correct, so this is inconsistent inside the same table.
- **Fix**: Swap the four mappings (epsilon→U+03F5, varepsilon→U+03B5, phi→U+03D5, varphi→U+03C6) and add a directed test comparing `ParseMath(L"\\phi\\varphi\\epsilon\\varepsilon").root.children[0].text` to the KaTeX code points.
- **Verifier (, confirmed)**: RichMarkdown source `$\phi(x) \ne \varphi(x)$` renders the first symbol as φ (U+03C6) and the second as ϕ (U+03D5), i.e. the two glyphs swapped relative to KaTeX/TeX; likewise `$\epsilon$` renders ε (U+03B5) and `$\varepsilon$` renders ϵ (U+03F5). Documents that use both forms with distinct meaning (common in physics) display the opposite variant from every other Markdown/TeX viewer. [...]

### status-truth-20

**MathNode/MathExpression and FlowchartNode/FlowchartEdge suppress implicit moves by defaulting the copy operations, forcing deep copies in the parsers**  
`include/DxUi/RichMath.h:35` · architecture · minor · confirmed (: confirmed) · plan: W2.12

- **What**: `MathNode(const MathNode&) = default; MathNode& operator=(const MathNode&) = default;` (RichMath.h:35-36, likewise MathExpression :50-51, FlowchartNode NativeFlowchart.h:68-69, FlowchartEdge :80-81) disable the implicit move constructor/assignment. Every `std::move` in the math parser (NativeMath.cpp:206, 220, 233, 401, 406, 412, 416) and every `std::vector<MathNode>` regrowth therefore copies the whole subtree (`text` plus `children`), making a 512-node formula cost O(nodes x depth) string/vector copies. If this is a deliberate guard against the MSVC Debug noexcept-move iterator-proxy termination recorded in the plan, the header does not say so; the test static\_assert (Scope.Tests.RichSemanticDocument.cpp:48) only pins the non-noexcept default constructor.
- **Failure**: Parsing a deep `\frac{\frac{...}}{...}` tower copies each inner tree once per enclosing level; under allocation-failure injection the extra copies also multiply the failure points that the sweeps must cover. A maintainer adding `std::move` believes it is cheap and it is not.
- **Fix**: Either add throwing (non-noexcept) move operations explicitly, or document in each header why moves are suppressed and reference the Debug proxy failure packet, so the pattern is not copied blindly into new object types.
- **Verifier (, confirmed)**: Maintainability/documentation finding, minor. MathNode/MathExpression (RichMath.h:35-36, :50-51) and FlowchartNode/FlowchartEdge (NativeFlowchart.h:68-69, :80-81) deliberately suppress implicit moves to honor the Core\_PerformanceAndResources.md:58-59/:772-773 rule against noexcept MSVC Debug proxy allocation, but neither header says so, and the same unexplained idiom appears on 16 types. [...]

### tests-engine-22

**Concurrent snapshot tests do not guarantee reader/owner overlap**  
`Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichDocument.cpp:225` · test-gap · minor · confirmed (: confirmed) · plan: T3

- **What**: TestRichDocumentConcurrentSnapshotReads spawns a reader doing 512 Slice/IsBoundary iterations while the owner does 256 Reset/ReplaceSelection/Undo/Redo cycles; TestRichSourceChangeConcurrentEmptyAndMovedValues (RichSourceChanges.cpp:174-191) does the same with 1024/128. There is no barrier, so on a fast machine one side typically finishes before the other starts; the tests prove immutability of values but almost never exercise true overlap, and MSVC builds have no TSan to catch a data race that ASan would miss.
- **Failure**: A regression where Document::Reset frees a shared piece buffer still referenced by an older SourceSnapshot would show up only if the reader touches it after the free; with the current ordering it usually does not, and the test passes.
- **Fix**: Use std::latch/std::barrier so both threads start together, loop until each has completed N iterations after the other started, and keep the ASan-run variant; optionally add a per-piece refcount assertion in Debug.
- **Verifier (, confirmed)**: Both concurrent tests (Scope.Tests.RichDocument.cpp:215-242, Scope.Tests.RichSourceChanges.cpp:165-191) prove value immutability but not concurrent access: the owner's first call is Reset, which starts a new lineage (DxUi.RichDocument.cpp:393) and clears history, so after round 0 the owner never again touches memory shared with the reader's snapshot, regardless of thread timing. Better-scoped fix (tests only): (1) start both sides on a std::latch(2) arrive\_and\_wait; [...]

`Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichTextEditor.cpp:538` · bug · minor · disputed (: refuted; : confirmed) · plan: T3

- **What**: TestRichTextEditorNativeMessageSchedulingAndFailure proves 'failed native work stays unavailable without a self-invalidating repaint loop' by pumping for 40 ms (`idleUntil = GetTickCount64() + 40u`, 538-543) and checking `editor->calls == failedCalls`. The same `GetTickCount64()+deadline / Pump / Sleep(1)` loop is re-typed at RichTextEditor.cpp:525-534, RichTextAccessibility.cpp:635-640, 1084-1089, 1472-1477, 1582-1587, RichSemanticResources.h:114-126, RichTextInput.cpp:134-144 and 203-214 although Controls.Tests.DxUiTestHelpers.h:147 already provides WaitForDxUiEventWithSentMessagesUntil.
- **Failure**: A regression that re-invalidates on the host's periodic Tick (e.g. caret blink at 500 ms, see TestRichTextEditorScrollAndHostClock) after a failed PrepareContent passes the 40 ms window, so the 'no polling loop' contract is not protected; on a loaded ASan runner the 12 s deadlines (527, 635, 1084) can also flake.
- **Fix**: Assert `window.Host().DebugGetInvalidateCount()` unchanged and that no WM\_PAINT is queued (PeekMessage PM\_NOREMOVE) after the failed turn instead of a timed idle; add one `PumpUntil(fixture, predicate, deadline)` helper to DxUiTestHelpers.h and use it everywhere.
- **Verifier (, refuted)**: The claimed wrong outcome ("the no-polling-loop contract is not protected; a 50 ms+ timer or next-Tick regression passes") is defeated by the actual host code and fixture state. (1) The only self-reschedule mechanism after a failed PrepareContent is ControlHost::Invalidate() -> InvalidateRect, gated on \_contentPreparationContinues, which PreparePendingPageLayout sets false whenever a control returns FAILED (DxUi.Controls.cpp:2310-2313, 2393-2394; WindowHost.cpp:4011-4018). `Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichDocumentLayout.cpp:447` · performance · minor · refuted (refute: uncertain; reproduce: refuted) · plan: not scheduled (refuted)

- **What**: TestRichDocumentLayoutWholeGeometryOracle (447-474) runs CompareDocumentGeometry with stride 1 for 4 endings x 2 modes x 2 widths x 2 directions on a ~2.2k-unit document; each position issues ~10 DirectWrite queries (Caret, ToSource x2, Prev/NextCluster, DeletionRange x2, LineBoundary x2, DisplayUnit) plus 41 visible-range/hit-test rows and 23 Range calls, roughly 0.7M DWrite calls. - **Verifier (refute, uncertain)**: Each Debug/ASan EditorControls run spends an unmeasured but plausibly significant time in the stride-1 whole-geometry oracle (16 configurations x ~2.2k positions). The 216 PNG writes are deliberate evidence retention, not waste. No measured duration supports "minutes" or "dominates", and the full PrePush/CI matrix still runs these tests.
- **Existing tests**: TestRichDocumentLayoutWholeGeometryOracle, TestRichDocumentLayoutWarpRasterOracle, TestRichDocumentLayoutLongHeightAndChunkBudget (Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichDocumentLayout.cpp:900-905)

### tests-host-17

**Native host has no post-device-loss pixel/geometry recovery check, unlike the embedded suite**  
`Tests/Controls/Scope.Tests.RichTextEditor.cpp:483` · test-gap · minor · confirmed (refute: confirmed; reproduce: confirmed) · plan: T3

- **What**: Native device loss is exercised only as cancellation: TestRichTextEditorNativeCooperativePreparation (482-486) and NativeLosslessMarkdownPreparation (1117-1118) call DebugSimulateDeviceLoss and assert pending work is retired and source intact, then continue without capturing. The embedded suite verifies recovery observably: ReplaceDevice followed by Composite/Read/PatternPixels (RichTextResources.h:1414-1429) and selection/history after replacement (2382-2393). DebugCaptureBitmap is available and used by the native image and diagram raster tests (886, 1060), so the gap is cheap to close.
- **Failure**: A native resource-generation bug that rebuilds glyph chunks but keeps a stale decoded-image or diagram bitmap bound to the lost device (DXGI\_ERROR\_DEVICE\_REMOVED on next draw, or black placements) passes EditorControls because no native test draws after DebugSimulateDeviceLoss.
- **Fix**: In TestRichTextEditorNativeImagePixels and NativeDiagramSelectionRaster, insert DebugSimulateDeviceLoss, RenderEditor, DebugCaptureBitmap and re-run PatternPixels/SelectionPixels; assert TakePendingImageRequests stays empty (held pixels reused).
- **Verifier (refute, confirmed)**: Native tests render after a simulated device loss, but only plain text or math, and never compare pixels. Image recovery goes through the shared ControlHost::DiscardDeviceResources path and is pixel-checked in Embedded. Diagram recovery after a device or factory replacement is not pixel-checked in either host, so a defect that drew stale factory geometry would escape both suites.
- **Existing tests**: TestEmbeddedImageResources (Tests/Embedded/Scope.Tests.RichTextResources.h:1414-1429); TestRichTextEditorNativeCooperativePreparation (Scope.Tests.RichTextEditor.cpp:482-498); TestWindowHostExplicitWarpPolicyRejectsConflictsAndSurvivesDeviceLoss (Scope.Tests.NativeDevicePolicy.h)
